Trefs (villages) 
These settlements largely consist of thatch roofed, 
stone- or timber-walled, roundhouses. The smoke 
from fires inside filters through the thatch, and live-
stock is brought inside in colder months. The dirt 
floors are covered with dry grasses, rushes, and hay, 
and those within use beds made from straw covered 
with rough wool blankets. The entrances to huts will 
be scored with apotropaic marks to keep evil spirits at 
bay. For Pagans these will generally be triskeles, whilst 
for Christians a Cross is used. 
The residents are a mix of tenant farmers, Irish in some 
areas, and slaves. The livestock includes cattle, pigs, 
sheep, and goats, which are looked after by herdsmen. 
Oxen were kept for ploughing, asses for beasts of bur-
den and horses for human transport, whilst bees pro-
vided honey. Most trefs are surrounded by rough 
wooden palisades, some even with ditches. 
Outside the tref the ploughed fields are bounded by 
wattle fences or live hedges, and further out is the graz-
ing land. Crops include wheat, oats, rye, barley, and 
flax; these were kept in small, stilted granaries and in 
storage pits. 
There are no Inns, taverns, or shops in the Kingdoms of Gwynedd, although a blacksmith (Forge Func-
tion) will be found in a few trefs. Those looking for a safe place to stay and some food for the night 
can offer a quarter-day’s work in the fields in exchange for a day’s rations of food and water and a 
safe corner of a roundhouse to sleep. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
39 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Hillforts 
These fortifications usually command the high ground and consist of simple, but substantial, dry-stone 
walls surrounding a few roundhouses. Outside there is birch fencing for the corralling of livestock.  
Some hillforts are crumbling remains of Roman-era and Iron Age fortifications used by Britons, but many 
are still in use. Some are used for refuge in times of trouble, whilst others are occupied year-round.  
Abandoned hillforts are excellent choices for PC Strongholds (FLPG pg 158). 
Sacred Groves (Nemeta) 
The druid’s orgininal groves were destroyed by the Romans over 400 years ago, but since their departure, 
the druids have quietly been reinstating these Nemeta. The following passage reflects what the lay-person 
believes the groves are like: 
“no bird nested in the nemeton, nor did any animal lurk nearby; the leaves constantly shivered though no breeze 
stirred. Altars stood in its midst, and the images of the gods. Every tree was stained with sacrificial blood. the very 
earth groaned, dead yews revived; unconsumed trees were surrounded with flame, and huge serpents twined round the 
oaks. The people feared to approach the grove, and even the priest would not walk there at midday or midnight lest 
he should then meet its divine guardian.” Lucan, Pharsalia 
However, in reality, although the groves appear devoid of birdlife, although the wind does not appear to 
penetrate, yet the boughs and leaves move of their own accord, and although there is usually a rough stone 
altar, complete with cup and ring marks and a basin to catch entrails from sacrifices, the palisaded groves 
are places of calm and contemplation. 
Sacred Groves are Zone 1 sites with respect to Magic (see Power Zones (pg 156)). 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
40 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Stone Circles and Giant’s Graves 
The stone circles of Gwynedd, such as L5.3 Bryn Cader Faner, predate the 6th century by at least 2000 years. 
They were not raised by the druids, but they contain great power and here the veil between the world and 
Annwn is thinner than elsewhere.  Druids approach these sites with a mix of reverence and caution. 
Giant’s graves on the other hand are literally the graves of giants. The remains underneath the cyclopean 
blocks have long since turned to crystals (see Beings of Magic (pg. 145)), but like stone circles the residual 
magic may result in a thinning of the veil in these locations.    
Stone Circles and Giant’s Graves are Zone 1 sites with respect ot Magic (see Power Zones (pg 156)). 
Hermitages and Cenobitic Sites 
By this age what were once rough, simple dwellings where an ascetic hermit might live have begun to 
develop into full monastic sites. In Gwynedd most hermitages are simply places where a single Ascetic hides 
away from the world and contemplates the Christian God. However, there is one Cenobitic site, L3.10 Saint 
Cynog’s Monastery, in Arfon, where ascetics have come together into a community. Even Saint Cynog’s is 
simply a restored hill fort, with only a single high tower and no embellishments. 
Hermitages and Cenobitic sites are Zone 3 with respect to Magic (see Power Zones (pg 156)).. 
Former Roman Architecture 
Sancta Arcis 
A Roman fortlet built on the island of Sancta Insula,19 its sides are 44x67m rectangular, with three walls. 
The fourth side is open to the sea over a low cliff. It was abandoned by the Romans and recently refortified 
by Tobrecan y Nerth(C4.4), the Lyblæca Sorcerer.   
Caer Segeint/Segontium 
L3.2 Caer Segeint, in Arfon, is built on the remains of 
Segontium, an abandoned Roman Fort, which once 
housed 1000 infantry. The architecture here is in stark 
contrast to the Celtic building styles elsewhere in 
Gwynedd.  
The fort sits on high ground, overlooking the sea. A 
high stone wall, 155 x 126m, surrounds rectangular 
Roman stone buildings and remains in relatively good 
repair. Most of the buildings now are just walls and 
used to house livestock, but 1/3 of the original build-
ing have kept their clay roofs and are used by Cadwal-
lon’s court (details). Cadwallon has taken over the for-
mer praetorium, which consist of rows of tiled rooms 
surrounding a central courtyard. Beside this are the in-
complete hypocaustum baths, now used as a piggery. 
 
19 Sancta Arcis is “Holy Citadel” in Latin, later called Ynys Gybi, after Saint Cybi. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
41 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Caer Rhun and Canovium 
A slightly smaller fort than Caer Segeint, the Roman fort of Canovium housed perhaps 500 soldiers and its 
square-plan, 125x125m, stone-built remains are in much worse repair. The fort is now abandoned, with a 
surviving Celtic settlement from the old Roman vicus just to the north. See L3.3 Caer Rhun and Canovium. 
 
Roman Roads 
The road from Segontium to Canovium and thence eastward remains in reasonable repair, and travel on 
these roads are swift. The remaining roads have since fallen into disrepair and are choked with silt and 
weeds. 
Other Roman Features 
Outside of the forts and the roads, occasional signs of the Roman occupation can be seen, these include 
tumbledown Roman villas, fallen aqueducts, and the like, however, a former Roman irrigation system re-
mains in use at L5.7 Llyn Cwmystradllyn near L5.4 Braich y Gornel.  
This former Roman irrigation system is used to create catchwork water-meadows. The water-meadows 
serve to keep the fields damp but without standing water. As a result, animal feed can start growing earlier 
than otherwise, due to frost prevention, and the ground absorbs nutrients from the silt carried over it. 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
42 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Travel in the Kingdoms of Gwynedd 
Use the Journeying rules in FLPG pg 143-157, with the following modifications: 
The Hexmap here uses 3km hexes, so convert distances travelled by increasing the number of hexes 
travelled per quarter day in the Journeying rules by a factor of three. So, 6 Hexes per Quarter Day on 
foot in Open Terrain, and 3 Hexes per Quarter Day on Difficult Terrain. 
An adventurer can see their surrounding hexes with a high degree of accuracy, but estimating distances and 
hex contents are difficult20 farther out than that.  
Horses are used by the wealthy, and carts, wagons, whilst not common, are present. The famous Celtic 
War Chariots and Carnyx battle horns, however, have fallen out of use by this time. 
The one Roman Road running over the north the Kingdoms can be hiked at 9 hexagons per quarter day21 
or 15 hexagons if on Horseback or cart.  
Location Tables 
The Location Tables are displayed by Cantref. The details show a name, any notable NPCs (in bold if a 
Significant NPC and therefor statted), and estimate of population, a description, details of any plague that 
is present, and references to the real-world location if it exists. 
The right-hand column lists the Stronghold Functions (FLPG pg 158) that may be available to the PCs 
in each area. It depends on the PCs relationships with the locals as to whether they can avail themselves of 
these Functions (possibly for a fee). Also provided are whether the location is a Senex Vaie (pg. 170) zone 
and what its magical Power Zone (pg. 156) is, if relevant. 
Plague Exposure Rating – this indicates the chance that a PC will be unwittingly exposed to the Plague 
when dealing with an NPC at a location. For every NPC that the PCs are in close contact with, roll a D66. 
If the result is at the rating or higher, then count that player as Exposed per The Plague (pg. 32). If the PCs 
seek out a plague victim, then that counts as automatic Exposure. At Plague Ratings of 44 and lower the 
Plague is highly evident at that location and there are signs of recent burnings and doors of huts festooned 
with relevant religious symbols, denoting a family visited by the illness. 
Additional information - To help the GM find out more about locations, then, if there is a reference in 
the Coflein database, a hyperlink and the National Primary Record Number (NPRN) database number is 
provided in {} brackets. If using a hard copy, then the location of, say NPRN {93511}, can be found by 
going to (https://coflein.gov.uk/en/site/93511). If there is a Wikipedia page, then there is a hyperlink 
provided, like this: [wiki]. 
Senses: Listed in italics are three sense prompts for the GM to help describe the location in a compelling 
and memorable manner. 
Aspects, listed in bold purple, are features of the location with which the PCs may interact with in some 
way. This helps the GM create a dynamic environment, where the PCs can engage with it and demonstrate 
agency. 
 
20 One can see mountains from much farther away, but estimating distances and geographical relationships is much 
more difficult. 
21 I.e. 3 Forbidden Lands scale hexes per quarter.

Location Code / Name / Leader/ Capitol 
Afflogion is largely thin peninsula, punctuated by a few steep hills, and much of the western facing shore, 
is sea-cliff. What flat parts it does have is good farmland, and it is wealthier than its neighbour Dunoding, 
although not as rich as Arfon.

rocks before joining the surf. The singing of women as they mend nets. 
The village lacks a palisade, and its huts are huddled around the mouth of the Daron

The harsh call of an ancient raven, as it awkwardly soars on turbulent thermals. The clatter of small 
stones, dislodged by a careless step. The small of decay from a dead sheep, bones thrusting through 
holes in its pelt. 
Tre’r Ceiri has been abandoned for at least the last 50 years. A main inner wall up to 
4m high and partial outer wall are situated at the top of a 450m high hill. Within the 
main wall are 26 large roundhouses and 150 smaller irregular and sub-rectangular 
buildings. Most of the buildings’ turfed roofs have collapsed, though a few remain in 
place.  For more details see O15. Tre’r Ceiri and Oron (pg. 120).

abandoned houses | Wind whistling through gaps in stone walls.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
45 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Ynys Enlli rises out of the Hibernian Sea like a whale-back. Between it and the mainland lies the treacherous 
sound of Ffrydiau Caswennan24. When the tide is running it is three kilometres of roiling, river-like, sea-
water.  The island has not yet been denuded by sheep, and the lowland sections are thickly forested with a 
mix of oak, ash, hazel, birch, and willow. It is less than 3km long by 1km wide at its widest, with an area of 
less than 2 square kilometres. The cliffs abound with nesting seabirds, there are many sea caves (Ogof), and 
singing seals skulk in the bays.

L3 Arfon and L4 Ynys Mons 
Arfon Cantref is largely moorland, upland, and high mountains, so its farmers largely rely on transhumance, 
the seasonal movement of livestock between summer and winter pastures28. A significant proportion of its 
land area is the island of Ynys Mons. The Royal Court of Caer Segeint harks back to richer times under the 
Roman rule, and the one remaining usable Roman Road in Gwynedd runs between L3.2 Caer Segeint and 
L3.3 Caer Rhun and Canovium.    
L3 Arfon Cantref / Cadwallon Longhand ab Einion / Caer Segeint 
[L3.1] Bwbachod Nest | Bwbachod Nest | NPCs: Wraig Fawr(C5.8) (Bwbach leader) 
| 20 Bwbachod.   
A narrow entrance requiring sliding awkwardly over slippery, slimy rocks to enter. The drip of water 
on something metal. The sickly-sweet smell of rotting meat.   
The nest is a low cave full of many small crevices. The Bwbachod socialise around a 
fire pit in the one open area and sleep on dirty straw in the hidden recesses. In the far 
back of the cave in a bowl-shaped depression in the rock sits a hoard of 25 gold coins. 
Aspects: Water dripping into a stone bowl | Brush screen covering the entrance 
| Broken Celtic Shield hiding a small, unlocked chest holding Wraig’s gold.  
Root Cellar.

wallon Longhand(C3.5), Meddyf ferch Maeldaf (wife to Cadwallon), Edyth the Prim 
(Saxon concubine of Cadwallon), Balthildis(C3.2) (f Lyblæca advisor), Lluan(C3.9) (f War-
lord) | 400 residents29 and 40 slaves, Plague Rating 55. {93511}[wiki] 
Crowds thronging the narrow rectilinear streets, which stink of midden and worse. Raised voices, as 
an Ambaxtos confronts a poorly dressed Aillt farmer, apparently in dispute over taxes. A splendid 
horse neighs nervously as its finely dressed rider urges it through the miasma from a nearby tannery. 
Cadwallon’s Royal Court is a former walled Roman Fort (see Caer Segeint/Segontium, 
(pg. 40)). There is also outside the walls a small dry-stone oratory dedicated to St Peblig 
{43801}, built outside the walls and over a burned out Temple to Mithras. The oratory 
uses corbelled construction on a square plan, with its walls curving up to form the 
shape of an upturned boat.  
Aspects: Rectangular Roman, tile roofed buildings with hypocausts | busy mar-
ketplace | Crabby Uchelwr accepting Renders (taxes).

Canovium: The clatter of shattered roof tiles under foot. The clanging of a broken door in the wind. 
The warbling of swallows as they swoop in and out of nests in the eaves.  
The square stone fort, sited to protect the lowest fording point of the Conwy River, is 
125m on a side. It previously housed hereditary Limitanei troops, after the Roman 
forces formally withdrew 100 years ago. These hereditary troops finally abandoned 
Canovium, and now only the former vicus to the north is occupied. The remains of a 
headquarters building, commanding officer's house, granaries, and barrack blocks are 
in poor shape, but some still have their roofs. 
Aspects: Roman Buildings with damaged roofs | Pigs kept in an abandoned 
barracks | Holes in the defensive walls.

old cistern in Canovium.  
Valuables: Also hidden with the Artefacts is a leather pouch with 100 silver coins. 
Caer Rhun: The laughter of children playing hide and seek. The ringing of metal on metal as a 
blacksmith works at a forge. The creak of wood, the clop of hoof, and the rasp of stone on stone, as an 
ox turns a mill. 
The former vicus to Canovium, features rectangular Roman buildings arrayed along 
the old Roman road leading to L3.2 Caer Segeint.

Below the Cairn, after climbing down31 a sheer east-facing, cliff, one finds the cave lair

the inner enclosure. She now protects the place as a Grim (Beings of Annwn (pg. 146)). 
Anyone attacking a monk on the site will find the Grim appears to protect him within 
1D6 rounds.   
The scriptorium here includes the Mystic Gospel of Mark, the apocryphal Acts of the 
Apostles33, and a Llatin translations of the magic-medical book Cyranides.

Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
The Island of Ynys Mons is separated from mainland Arfon by a thin channel. The land here is very fertile, 
much grain is grown, and the farmers who make this home return rich taxes to Cadwallon, via his vassal 
Tobrecan.  
L4 Ynys Mons / Tobrecan on behalf of Cadwallon / Santca Insula 
[L4.1] Aberffraw | Tref on Ynys Mons | NPCs: Gofan ap Gareth(C4.1) (Scáthach’s(C4.3) 
2nd in command) | 10 warriors, 10 huts, 40 residents, 5 slaves, Plague Rating 44. 
{15012}[wiki] 
The slap of waves on a small wooden pier. The hoarse screeching of herring gulls. The smell of fish 
pervading all things. 
Aberfraw is the main access point for Ynys Mons, where Tobrecan has decreed visitors 
must transit. Here he has a small garrison of warriors ready to greet and assess arrivals. 
Aspects: Wary residents who keep to themselves | A crude bell hung from a tree 
bole in the centre of the village | a small grain silo which has been torn open 
and is empty. 
Artefacts: Gofan holds a Sonio Grwyn rattle (A15) 
Field, Mill, Pas-
ture, Pigsty, Root 
Cellar, Sheep-
fold, Well. 
[L4.2] Bryn Celli Ddu (BRIN KE-chli THEE)| Neolithic Burial Chamber | 0 Resi-
dents. {93827}[wiki] 
The rustle of grass in a light wind. The smell of lavender filling nostrils. A hum, so low in tone that 
it is more felt than heard, which seems to come from everywhere and nowhere. 
Bryn Celli Ddu (Mound of the Dark Grove) is and ancient sacred site, which began as 
a henge, then a passage grave, then covered over with a large mound. The mound, 
which sits in a large clearing, is over 25m in diameter, banked within kerbstones and 
outside, a surrounding ditch. The one entrance on the east is flanked by two tall stand-
ing stones, and the passage within is sided with vertical stone slabs. This passage opens 
into a small central chamber within the mound. A 1m tall, round-topped pillar stands 
in the chamber entrance, and beyond there is a large rectangular stone that floats un-
supported 1m above a dark pit in the floor. This pit is the Well of the World.  
For more details see O22. Interception, (pg. 138). 
Aspects: Trees cleared systematically to southwest | Decayed head on spike 
forming part of large Ghost Fence34 (8D Fear Attack) | Nearby Stream leaching 
orange silt. 
Shrine. 
 
Senex Vaie

[L4.3] Llwyn o Gerrig (The Grove of Stones) | Sacred Grove on Ynys Mons which 
encapsulates the small lake of Llyn Cerrig Bach | NPCS: Indeg and Tegno (f druids) | 
2 druid residents, Plague Free. {401097}[wiki] 
The rich semen like smell of some kind of pollen. The splash of water from a small spring. The sound 
of the breeze in the upper canopy evoking the feeling of a chanted hymn.   
In the centre of the grove, on the shores of a small lake, stand six large pottery urns. 
These hold the cremated remains of the Druids who were massacred by the Romans 
in AD 60-61. Since long before the coming of the Romans, Llyn Cerrig Bach has been 
used to deposit votive offerings to the gods. 
Aspects: Large boulders intermixed with huge old oaks | Tame deer sheltering 
within the bounds | Rusty Axe embedded in a dead tree trunk. 
Artefacts: The Greal Sanctaidd (A07) lies in the mud at the bottom of the Llyn, having 
recently been made an offering by the druids when Tobecran gained power here. The 
Druids here know of its power to assist in navigating the Senex Vaie.  
Shrine.

[L4.4] Pant-Y-Saer | Tref | 6 huts, 25 residents and 5 slaves. Plague Free. {93857} 
The bellowing of oxen, complaining as they pull a plough. Eyes watering from smoke rising from a 
kiln being used to preserve today’s catch. A cut-off cry of pain as a slave is struck by their owner.  
The tref looks out over the sea and consists of a large oval stone enclosure with two 
large stone roundhouses and several smaller wattle roundhouses. Fishing is the main 
form of subsistence. Nearby there is an ancient burial chamber {95529}. 
Aspects: Children running screaming in delight through the village | Wing-
clipped ducks wandering | An overflowing midden.

The popping sound of bubbles bursting, as an oar catches in an eddy. The pungent, sulphury smell of 
the sea. The gargling sound of a cormorant drying its wings whilst perched on nearby rocks.  
Here the water boils around the small islets in the straight when the tide is in flood. 
This is the shortest distance to cross to Ynys Mons, but those attempting to cross will 
find it treacherous (see O07. To Ynys Mons, (pg. 99)). 
Aspects: Rocky and almost submerged islets | Long strands of thick kelp |

[L4.6] Sancta Arcis | Fort and Tref | NPCs: Tobrecan y Nerth(C4.4) (m Lyblæca Sor-
cerer), Scáthach(C4.3) (f warlord), Boadhagh ab Brydw (m Aillt farmer) | 20 warriors, 
fort, 10 huts, 40 residents and 15 slaves. Plague Free. {15607}[wiki] 
The smacking sound of waves striking the low cliff. The sound of snapping and crashing, as a group 
of workers fell mature trees on the avenue leading to the fort entrance. The soundless, smooth opening 
of the fort oak door.   
Sancta Arcis is a former Roman fortress, built to protect the area from marauding 
Hibernian pirates and invaders. It itself is on a small island, Sancta Insula35, and sepa-
rated from Ynys Mons by a narrow channel. Tobrecan has taken the site and refur-
bished it for his use. Outside the fort a small Tref has grown up. For details see O10. 
An Audience at Sancta Arcis, (pg. 104).  
Aspects: Sea Winds making footing difficult on walls | A fisherman bringing in 
a large catch | Aillt tending a walled garden. 
Artefacts: Tobrecan holds the sword Bitor (A01) and the Codex Climaci Rescriptus 
(A03). Scáthach holds a Sonio Grwn rattle (A15).

The smashing roar of the surf crashing into the rocky shore. Seabirds squawking as they divebomb 
those who stray too close to their nests. The feel of sea-spray drying on the skin. 
This small and wild island, just off the main island of Ynys Mons, has recently been

L5 Dunoding 
Dunoding Cantref lies along the sea, wrapped around the north end of the Bae Ceredigion. Much of its 
upland in the west is dominated by peat, so arable farming is limited, and it is less wealthy than its neighbour, 
Arfon. 
L5 Dunoding Cantref / Dingad ab Eifion / Gaer Dolbenmaen  
[L5.1] Arddlech | Tref | NPCs: Gareth the Just (headman) | 25 huts, 120 residents, 
20 slaves. Plague Rating 66. [wiki] 
The rich odour of peat-smoke rising through roundhouse thatch. The howling and barking of local dogs 
as they race through the village. The crunch of footsteps on earthy paths. 
Arddlech is a large tref, situated near the sea and nearby river mouth. It is palisaded 
and many of the huts are of drystone construction with turf roofs. 
Aspects: Lookout point with views of the sea | Wood gathered in a high place 
as a beacon | Slippery steep tracks down to the flat ground below. 
Bakery, Field, 
Forge, Garden,

dents, 10 slaves. Plague Rating 66. {302692}[wiki] 
The thwack of an axe being used to split wood. The smell of freshly baked bread. The sweet smell of 
manure being spread on the field. 
Ardudwy is a medium sized tref. It has no palisade and most of the huts are of wooden 
construction with thatched roofs. 
Aspects: Closely arranged Roundhouses | Plenteous Garden | Suspicious Chil-
dren.

The musty, earthy smell, of the peat underfoot. The almost palpable silence that descends when the 
wind dies, and the birdsong suddenly fades. The course surface of the stones, bedecked with lichens and 
mosses. 
This ancient stone circle is unusual with its array of 30 closely spaced, outward facing, 
2m-long, spikelike stones.  
Aspects: Jagged stone barbs arrayed like defensive spikes | Central offering 
stone with shallow depression | Views down to the coast.

[L5.5] Ffridd Isaf ring cairn | Shrine | 0 Residents {532546} 
The creak of large wooden crosses swaying in the breeze. The smell of burnt meat. The buzz of disturbed 
flies, which rise swarming into the air at one’s approach.  
This former pagan site now is sacred to the Christians.  Christians emboldened by 
Abad Cadfan’s preaching have taken to leaving Offerings on the ring cairn. This in-
volves burning livestock alive and leaving crosses in the surrounding mud. This has 
tainted nearby L5.7 Llyn Cwmystradllyn, and a black trail of ooze leads from the cairn 
to the Llyn. 
Aspects: Crude wooden crosses erected around the stone circle | Central stone 
covered in burnt offerings | Sluggish trickle meandering towards the llyn. 
Shrine.

[L5.6] Gaer Dolbenmaen |Royal Court of Dunoding Cantref and Hillfort | NPCs: 
Rhi Dingad(C5.5), Ystradwel(C5.7) (Wife of Dingad), Elno(C5.6)(m druid counsellor), 
Casanauth(C5.4) (m Uchelwr noble), Alcwn(C5.3) (m warlord), Conan ab Conan (m 
Aillt Farmer), Drust (m Pictish Slave), Huctia (f Pictish Slave), Afrandreg(C5.1) (f bard) 
| 100 huts, 300 residents and 20 slaves, Plague Rating 66. {95306}{95304}[wiki] 
The clashing of practice spears on shields, as Ambaxtos train under a sergeant. The shouts and calls 
of guards from the walls and palisade. The rumbling of a cart being used to bring the taxes in from 
the surrounding area.   
Gaer Dolbenmaen consists of a pair of two small hillforts36, each just 20m diameter, 
flanking a larger, wooden palisaded, community lower in the valley, and which straddles 
the ford of Afon Dwyfor37. This controls the main trade route from the south through 
to L3.2 Caer Segeint in Arfon. Nearby there is a shrine dedicated to Nodens, which is 
a small grotto converted from a Roman nymphaeum. 2 km up-river there are the tum-
bledown remains of a roman fort, Pen Llys Tyn38 {301056}. 
Dingad’s hall is an imposing stone roundhouse with a high thatched roof.   
Aspects: Impressive Wood Palisade | Steep fall from the two overlooking Forts 
| Shallow Ford.  
Artefacts: Afandreg holds the Pibgorn of Glamour (A11)

[L5.7] Llyn Cwmystradllyn | Lake | 0 Residents {401212} 
The water surface ripples seeming too thick for normal water. Bubbles bursting and releasing a musty 
taint. The lack of any birdcall or sight of any bird alighting on the water. 
Previously known for its beauty, the waters of the Llyn are now black with the taint 
from the nearby defiled L5.5 Ffridd Isaf ring cairn. As a result, an Afanc (Beings of 
Annwn (pg. 146)) has broken through the veil from Annwn and now resides in its 
murky waters. At the southwest end there is an original, but still used, Roman Irrigation 
System, including sluice gates, channels, drains, and field ridges, which support water-
meadows in the fields below (See Other Roman Features, (pg. 41).  
Aspects: Turbid black water | Ill-maintained and crumbling dam walls | River-
stone edged beach.

The howl of the wind whipping through rock crevices. The grating and clacking sound of rocks shifting 
underfoot. A far-off cry of some strange bird, lost in an instant to the heavy wind. 
The veil between worlds is thin-to-breaking here, and a pack of Cŵn Annwn (Beings of 
Annwn (pg. 146)) dutifully guard the approach. They will attack anyone who lingers

Silver worth of Agardite Crystals.

L6 Rhos 
Rhos cantref lies to the east of the river Cynwy. It is a small sub-kingdom compared to Arfon.  
L6 Rhos / Owain Whitetooth / Din Arth 
[L6.1] Cyngreawdr Fynydd40 | Abandoned copper mines | NPCs: Emrys 
Nofather(C6.2) | A clan of Cythraul live here, Plague Free. {33779}[wiki] 
The creak, as decaying wooden supports shift, a scattering of dust fall, and then silence. Echoing 
footsteps become apparent, first loud, then barely audible, then loud again. High pitched cries of many 
bats, then flapping forms overhead, then silence. 
Copper has been mined here since long before the Romans arrived, and the passages 
run for more than five km. The sea has broken through into some lower passages; 
when the tide rises these can be cut off. For more details see O14. Finding Emrys, (pg. 
116). 
Aspects: Dark side passages which quickly squeeze down to become almost im-
passable in places | Guttering torch illuminates a deep vertical pit | Ancient 
broken mining tools provide an improvised weapon. 
Artefacts: Emrys holds the Lleuad Cilgant (A09) moon sickle and in the bottom of the 
mine is the Brooch of Cúchulainn (A05) 
Valuables: Hidden in the mine by a previous miner is a cache of 100 units of smelted 
Copper (worth 1000 copper = 10 gold coins, and counts as 2 Heavy Items + 1 Light 
Item) 
Mine 
[L6.2] Din Arth | Royal Court of Rhos Cantref and Hillfort | Rhi Owain White-
tooth(C6.3), Efrog of the Stake(C6.1) (m druid) | 50 huts, 150 residents and 20 slaves, 
Plague Free. {93326}[wiki] 
A Bard strikes up a song, and the hubbub and murmur of the court falls away in respectful silence. 
The clash and ring of sword on sword as a practice duel turns more serious. The smell of roasting meat 
on a spit. 
Din Arth41 is the Royal Court of Owain Whitetooth. It is smaller than some of the 
other Royal Courts in Gwynedd, but the aging Owain has decorated it with fine tro-
phies, cunningly worked furniture, and intricate wall-hangings. 
Aspects: Wattle screen blocks the view of much of the court from the central area 
where deals are made | Weapons rack filled with seaxes, spears, and a few pol-
ished swords | Silver tipped auroch horn instrument hanging from a wall.

Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Significant NPCs 
Named NPCS are those mentioned by name in the setting material. These are separated into Significant 
and Minor NPCs. Significant NPCs are those listed in Bold in one or more Obstacles and for which 
there is a full description and stats provided here. Minor NPCs, on the other hand, are those listed in the 
text or shown in the Relationship Maps with only basic information, sometimes just a name, gender, and 
profession.  
Significant NPCs are listed here, alphabetically by region in cross-referenced tables, and these can be 
printed off and referred to quickly with all the salient details in 
one spot. 
For each region a Relationship Map is provided. These give at 
glance information on how the Significant and Minor NPCs re-
late, including whether they trust each other and sometimes the 
basis for the relationship. Significant NPCs have their ref listed as 
(CX.X). 
After their Name, Gender, Profession/Position, Culture, Reli-
gion, Location and Obstacles, the information provided for Sig-
nificant NPCs uses a common shorthand, called here 
“DeMASKQuE+”. Each one-line aspect is designed to quickly 
give insight to the GM as to the NPC’s personality, description 
and equipment. First is a Description, then the NPC’s Motiva-
tion, then three Adjectives, then Secrets/Knows, any Quirks, un-
usual Equipment and then any other relevant information. The 
NPC’s relevant Forbidden Lands stats are then listed adjacent.

[C1.4] Oron, Ghost, former High Druid of Britain. L1.4 Tre’r Ceiri.  
➢ 
De: First shows his presence by a cold spot in the room, then by a low 
moaning, and finally by showing himself. He appears as an emaciated 
man, dressed in rags that were once fine robes. His eye sockets seem 
deeper than normal, his pallid skin seems leprous, and his voice mani-
fests weariness.     
➢ 
M: Guilt 
➢ 
A: Conscientious, Distracted, Irritable 
➢ 
S/K: Knows that Cadfan(C3.4) has created a Grim, that the Eaglestone’s 
power can be released to the land by placing the amulet in the well in 
L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu, and that Caer Sidi can be accessed from L3.7 Llyn 
Gwynant through the shepherd who lives there (O15. Tre’r Ceiri and 
Oron, (pg 120 ). 
➢ 
Qu: Believes it is still the time of the Roman Occupation 
➢ 
E: none. 
Oron was the Head Druid of Britain at the time of the Roman Campaign of 
extermination of the Druids in 60-61 AD (led by the Roman governor Gaius 
Suetonius Paulinus). He escaped the massacre of his people, and afterward 
he wandered the land, his mind broken by guilt and the sights he had seen. 
He ended up in Tre’r Ceiri, where he lived for several more years before 
dying in 70 AD. The presence of his ghost led to the decline and eventual 
abandonment of the hillfort, and he has haunted it ever since.

Briton, Pagan. L3.6 Dinas Affaraon. 
➢ 
De: 21, female, medium height, and very sinewy build. Dark auburn 
hair frames course features, and the left-hand side of her face is a mass 
of burn scars. The right-hand side, on the other hand, is covered in 
intricate blue tattoos. Like her face, the left-hand side of her body is 
also a mass of burn scars, whilst the right-hand side is heavily tattooed. 
She wears a simple linen tunic under a heavy bear-fur and around her 
neck is a dirty necklace of bear teeth on a gut string. 
➢ 
M: Bodhmall 
➢ 
A: Curious, Determined, Feral 
➢

the Blight. 
➢

➢ 
E: Seax, Dagger, Furs and Hide armour, 6 silver. 
Angharad was taken in by Bodhmall(C3.3) two years ago. The young girl had 
been a slave of the Gaels in Hibernia where her owner would pit her against 
bears in a fighting ring. Bodhmall used her influence to force her owner to 
sell the young girl to him, and she brought Angharad over with her when the 
Druidess came to these lands.  
Anghard is prickly and difficult with anyone besides Bodhmall, is jealous 
when Bodhmall pays attention to others, and is fiercely loyal to the Arch-
druid.

of Gwynedd, Gael, Pagan. L3.6 Dinas Affaraon. 
➢ 
De: Late-20s, female, slim and tall with a wide face, long, un-tonsured, 
braided red hair and with milky-white, blind eyes. She wears black linen 
robes, pinned at the shoulders with two elaborate gold brooches. 
Around her neck is a magnificent, heavy gold torc. 
➢ 
M: The Land

A: Businesslike, Animated, Compassionate

➢

Ingredients: bear claws (3), raven claws (3) 
Bodhmall was born in Hibernia and brought to Gwynedd to take on the 
mantle of Arch Druid at the age of 16. Note: She can see when using True 
Sight, and when in animal form she has the normal sight of the animal.

Wits 4, Empathy 4.

[C3.4] [Abad] Cadfan ab Eneas Ledewig, Male, Abbot of St Cynog’s 
Monastery, Gaul, Christian. L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery. 
➢ 
De: 25-years-old, male, short and stocky with a round face and fore-
head tonsure. He wears a brown wool cassock and cowl with an osten-
tatious gold crucifix on a gold chain prominently displayed.  
➢ 
M: The Mission  
➢ 
A: Intense, Earnest, Epicurian 
➢ 
S/K: Secret - He sacrificed a Pagan Woman by burying her alive under 
the foundations of St Cynogs to create a Grim. 
➢ 
Qu: Nasal voice. 
➢ 
E: Gold Cross. Saint Cynog’s Gilded Armilla (A14) 
Cadfan has travelled from Brittany, via Tours, in Gaul, where, influenced by 
the monastery at Ligugé Abbey, he has decided to create his own religious 
community, which he has dedicated to Saint Cynog.44 Cadfan recovered the 
saint’s famous gilded, iron armilla, which is held in veneration in the monas-
tery. From his readings of his apocryphal Acts of the Apostles, Cadfan be-
lieves the Druids are followers of Simon Magus and has vowed to remove 
them from Gwynedd. 
A: Strength 3, Agility 2, 
Wits 5, Empathy 4

[C3.5] Cadwallon Longhand (kad-OO-AH-clon) ab Einion Yrth, Male, 
Ruler of Arfon, Briton, Pagan. L3.2 Caer Segeint. 
➢ 
De: 55 years old, male, medium height and build. His reddish-brown 
hair is streaked with grey, and he wears it long down to his shoulders. 
His blue-grey eyes seem to sparkle with excitement. He wears a blue 
linen tunic, linen leggings, and soft leather shoes, all obviously im-
ported. He wears a small gold fasces45 on a leather thong around his 
neck.  
➢ 
M: His People 
➢ 
A: Altruistic, Calculating, Mischeivous 
➢ 
S/K: He has sent his son Maelgwn(C3.10) to Cadfan(C3.4) for more than 
just to learn the ways of Christianity. He also expects his son to spy on 
the Christians. 
➢ 
Qu: Refers to Slaves as “our little helpers”.  
➢ 
E: fine sword, 4 gold coins, fine clothing.  
Cadwallon has ruled this kingdom for many years and now is looking to en-
sure it is in good hands after he is gone. His relationship with his wife, Med-
dyf ferch Maeldaf, is cordial but cool, however, his great love is his first con-
cubine, Edith the Prim. He confides in her in matters of State, and he con-
siders her opinions when making difficult choices.  
The Lyblæca Sorcercer, Tobrecan y Nerth(C4.4), has recently been instru-
mental in removing the Hibernian presence on Ynys Mons. In return Cad-
wallon has made him his vassal and gifted him control of the entire island on 
his behalf. However, he still doesn’t know whether he can trust Tobrecan. 
Cadwallon is half-brother to Owain Whitetooth(C6.3) and father to Mael-
gwn Gwynedd(C3.10).

is the restoration of the primacy of the Britons for these lands, and although 
he will appear friendly and helpful, he is not beyond betraying a confidence 
if it progresses his cause.  He does not hate Christianity, but he does work to 
slow its impact on his beloved land. 
Emrys understands the gods hate to be bored, so he works to ensure that 
they are amused – he feels it is this that has kept him safe these many years. 
He feels that the Christian God demands servile behaviour, purity, and grov-
elling and, above all, despises pleasure.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
78 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Obstacles 
The Dreams 
The dreams should start on the first night of the campaign and the third dream should not happen until 
after Obstacle O02 or O03 occurs. The name on the standing stone revealed by the third dream is written 
in Ogham and reads “bodhmall”. Any druids in the party will know this the Archdruid of Gwynedd, who 
resides in L3.6 Dinas Affaraon. If the party has no druid, then the PCs should know they can consult a 
local druid, or pass a Lore check, to decipher the writing and know her location. 
The First dream 
You wake with a start and look around at the others. It is still 
dark, but you are all awake. The dream is fading already but you 
do remember fragments. You were following a narrow stream up 
towards a crest in an idyllic landscape. Reaching the brow, you saw 
a magnificent stag. It stared at you and then dipped its head to point 
to a golden chalice at its feet.  
The Second dream 
Again, you wake, and you realise it was the same dream, only this 
time you remember a little more: You saw the chalice at the stag’s 
feet was full of bright red wine. The chalice fell into the stream, but 
instead of the water turning red, it became an inky, sludge-like, 
black. You looked up and all around the grass was withering and the land fell to a brown and desolate wasteland. The smell 
of sweet grass was replaced by an overpowering smell of decay. It did not seem odd that you were smelling things in this dream.  
The Third dream 
Like the first two, but now you remember more: …turning from the stag and the stream, you beheld a tall standing stone. A 
crow called, and then landed on this stone and held you with a bright glittering eye. It pecked at the stone along one vertical 
edge, and a series of horizontal slashes appeared under its beak. You realise the slashes form a name “ᚁᚑᚇᚆᚋᚐᚂᚂ” 
 
ᚁᚑᚇᚆᚋᚐᚂᚂ 
(Bodhmall) 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
80 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Timeline & Faction Map 
The world does not sit still whilst the PCs work to end The Blight. The Faction Timeline allows you to 
keep track of what is happening. Rather than monitoring dates, it simplifies the process by linking Obsta-
cles completed to Faction activities. Just tick off an empty slot every time an Obstacle is completed, and 
then present the world to the PCs. If the PC’s activities would affect these events, then take that into 
account also50. 
To reach the Resolution should take between 8 and 12 further Obstacles, after Obstacle 05. It may result 
in armed conflict between Cadwallon and Tobrecan. Use the table as is, adjust if desired, or ignore com-
pletely. 
# 
Obs 
Faction 
Activity 
T1

Monks 
Sends envoys to Cadwallon(C3.5) in L3.2 Caer Segeint to protest the desecra-
tion of L5.5 Ffridd Isaf ring cairn (1-in-6 chance that Cadwallon accepts them 
and declares the PCs Outlawed in Arfon) 
T2 
 
Dunoding 
Briton 
Sends spies into Afflogion to look for signs of secret collaboration between 
Einion(C1.3) and Cadfan(C3.4). Einion has been secretly collecting tithes from 
Dunoding Christians and his Ambaxtos warriors have been taking them to 
L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery (2-in-6 chance that the spies discover the tith-
ing of Dunoding Christians, and Dingad(C5.5) levies a Christian Tax, angering 
Afflogion and Cenobites)  
T3 
 
Druids 
Relenting to pressure from the Rhi, Owain Whitetooth(C6.3), the druids of 
Rhos begin raising a Wickerman with the intention of burning sacrifices to halt 
The Blight (3-in-6 chance that Bodhmall learns of this, and a power struggle 
begins within the order between her and Efrog of the Stake(C6.1). See T8).

Andronicus(C1.1) of Afflogion on the hilltop of L3.8 Mynydd Y Cilgwyn in 
Arfon. Their intention is to influence the Rhiau to work in a more coordinated 
manner in dealing with The Blight (1-in-6 chance that Cadwallon learns of 
the meeting and excludes Balthildis from his court)

Halts sending of Render (taxes) to Cadwallon(C3.5), declaring Ynys Mons in-
dependent of Arfon.

Tobrecan(C4.4). If not, then he sends a contingent of Ambaxtos to take and 
control L4.1 Aberffraw (significantly complicating access and egress of Ynys 
Mons).

Monks

Cynog’s Monastery (3-in-6 chance their request is approved and supply trains 
begin to run between Garn Boduan and Saint Cynogs) 
Additionally, if Maelgwn(C3.10) has attacked the Cenobite Monks at St Cynog’s 
(O19), then an envoy is sent to Cadwallon(C3.5) demanding weregild for all 
Christian deaths. This causes an uproar as the news filters through Gwynedd. 
The existing neutral-to-poor relations between Arfon and the Cenobites and 
between Arfon and Afflogion now worsens significantly. 
T8

The power struggle between Bodhmall and Efrog(C6.1) comes to a head (2-in-
6 chance that Efrog is dominant and becomes the new Archdruid. In this case

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
82 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O01. Augury on Moel Yscydion 
Procedural Goal 
Introduce the setting and give the PCs a choice about their next move. 
Emotional Goal 
Start strong with immediate conflict and tough choices. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L5.8 Moel Yscydion, Dunoding 
See In Media Res… the beginning (pg 9). The PCs are escorting Dingad’s(C5.5) chief Druid, Elno(C5.6), to Moel 
Yscydion so that he can carry out an augury on behalf of the Rhi. The intent is to find a solution to the 
Blight, however, the giant’s grave cairn on the top of Moel Yscydian has been knocked down, dissipating 
the ritual’s power, and Cŵn Annwn hounds haunt the area. If you are going to narrate the following, then 
stop between each paragraph and “check in” with the players to ensure understanding and allow them to 
react. 
Arriving at the peak Elno turns and surveys the ground. “This is not right” he growls “the cairn has been 
disrupted”. What once must have been a tall oblong cairn, perhaps 5m long x 2m wide and consisting 
of blocks too large for a man or woman to lift, has been scattered over a wide area. In the centre 
remain a small pile of tiny bluish-green crystals, although the breeze is slowly blowing them over 
the cliff edge. “Huh, so there was a giant under here after all” says Elno as he points at the crystals. “Some 
Lyblæca will pay a pretty sum for those crystals, all that remains of Yscydion it appears”. 
Near the remains of the cairn the ground drops away into murk-filled abyss, however, a narrow 
neck of rock, only 1m wide, joins this summit and a sheer pillar, perhaps 4m diameter, with a 
sound, flat top.  
Elno casts about, sighs, and then says, “This may not work, but nevertheless I must attempt the ritual.”  
After carefully laying a large circle of salt around the cairn site, Elno barks “I need quiet now. Whatever 
occurs, do not enter this circle”. He then scrambles over the large, scattered blocks, stops in the centre, 
spins three times sunwise, and sinks to his knees. Pulling out a mass of stringy, fishy-smelling 
viscera from his pouch, he casts it to the ground, turns his head skywards, and commences to chant 
in an unfamiliar, guttural language. His eyes roll back into his head, he begins to give out ululating 
shrieks; presently it begins to rain. Time passes. 
More howling is heard, but it becomes fainter before fading away completely (Possible Lore Test to see if 
the PCs realise this means the Hounds are coming closer). The hounds stay out of sight until after Elno 
starts the ritual, but then appear out of the mist (SHORT range) one hound and then a second silently 
approach and circle the cairn (NEAR range to the salt line), initially staying away from the salt line, as if 
girding themselves to cross it. Eventually they will cross the line to ARM’s LENGTH range of Elno and 
proceed to attack anyone within the salt circle.  
The total number of hounds should reflect a challenge, but not one where the PCs feel in real peril. A good 
start might be two hounds, with the PCs wondering what has become of the third they heard earlier (See 
In Media Res… the beginning (pg 9)). If two are too easy, then a third, or more can arrive later. 
The pillar top is an obvious defensible position, as there the PCs cannot be flanked by the hounds.  


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
83 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Anyone entering the circle whilst Elno is enacting the ritual will be assaulted by visions of otherworldly of 
monsters and demons, as they see through the thinning of the veil and into Annwn (Fear Attack, 5D, FLPG 
pg 112). 
Dilemma  
The druid Elno needs protecting from the hounds, but whilst attempting the augury he becomes wrapped 
up in his ritual and heedless to the danger. The nearby pillar top is an obvious defensible point, but for the 
PCs to retreat there will leave Elno undefended. The hounds are hesitant to cross the salt circle but appear 
intent on slowly approaching the druid and will eventually rush in to attack. 
Choices 
1. Protect Elno by attracting the hound to themselves such that they will be surrounded.  
or  
2. Break the ritual and drag Elno over the promontory to the pillar-top where the PCs can only be 
attacked by one hound at a time. 
Consequences 
1. If the ritual is interrupted, then the PCs find that Elno was unable to divine anything useful regard-
ing The Blight, but he does report Part 1 of his Visions (below).  He will refuse to re-try the ritual, 
shaken by the experience. 
2. If the ritual is completed, then in addition to the above, Elno will add the Part 2 aspect. 
Note: If the Hounds manage to wound Elno so that he cannot proceed, then they will disperse on their 
own. 
Elno’s Visions 
Vision Part 1.  Elno: “I saw you, plain as I see you now. You were searching for something. And I saw those three Saxon 
Traders who were in Dolbenmaen before we left on this task. Has something happened to them?” 
Vision Part 2. Elno: Part 1 + “Whilst in the trance I found myself raising high above the kingdom. It was beautiful…” 
at this point a tear trundles down Elno’s face. “But then I was shown every barren wife in the land. One by one. All 
that pain. All that loss. Stretching evermore into the future. It was horrible. However, there is one thing I noticed; there was 
not a single Christian amongst them. Why do you think that might be?”  
Rooting Interest 
1. The PCs fee and Dingad’s future patronage is contingent upon the PCs’ good performance. 
2. The PCs know that letting Elno be injured will reflect badly upon them in any future negotiations 
with druids. 
3. The centre of the destroyed cairn has 3D12 Silver worth of Agardite Crystals remaining (only worth 
this to a Lyblæca sorcerer) 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
84 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Next Steps 
The PCs only lead51 is the Saxon Traders who went missing an 8-day ago. They will need to return to L5.6 
Gaer Dolbenmaen to pursue this further.  
Travel:  
As the PCs pass either L5.1 Arddlech or L5.2 Ardudwy they see pyres burning on the outskirts. These are 
for locals who have succumbed to the plague. The purpose of this vignette is to introduce the PCs to the 
consequences of the plague. 
On their return and entrance into Dingad’s halls a Bard sings a song that presumes the PC’s success and 
giving thanks for their safe return. Dingad(C5.5) will thank the PCs and pay them 12 silver each if Elno is 
returned (mostly) in one piece, 6 silver if he maimed (lost limbs, etc), or nothing if Elno did not survive. 
Links to following Obstacles: 
Even the most basic investigations in L5.6 Gaer Dolbenmaen on the whereabouts of the missing Saxon 
Traders (Dougal (m), Anna (f), Isen (m)) will reveal that there were conflicting reports on the Traders’ 
movements. They had told many in the Dingad’s court that they intended to travel west to L1.3 Garn 
Boduan before returning here, but Barra Gwallgof, the old mad hermit in nearby L5.4 Braich y Gornel, has 
also told many in the court that he saw them travelling north into the mountains.  
1. If the Traders had travelled west, then they may have been taken by Outlaws who are known to 
plague that route (O01).  
2. If they had travelled north, then those in court believe they are most likely victims of the 
Bwbachod (Goblins)(O02) who have been reported in the area recently. 
Complications: 
1. Casanauth ab Cynon(C5.4), Dingad’s chief advisor, will privately approach the PCs and request 
that they deal with the Outlaws if they head west. He will offer the PCs 24 silver each should they 
return with proof of the deaths of the Outlaws (Motivation: Alcwn(C5.3), Dingad’s Ambaxtos has 
thus far avoided dealing with the issue for unstated reasons52). If the PCs choose to search north 
and ignore Casanauth he will be ill-disposed to help them in future, although he will become a clear 
political enemy if the PCs meet with the Outlaws and leave them as they find them.  
2. Subsequently, Caeth Áine(C5.2), Dingad’s Gael slave concubine, will approach the PCs, state she is 
aware of Casanauth’s request (Easiest reason for this is that she overheard the conversation), and 
ask that they leave the Outlaws unmolested (Motivation: her cousin Fintan is in the band). She 
will offer her future political support if the PCs leave the Outlaws alone.  
 
 
 
 
51 Note: If Elno is unable to pass on his information for any reason, then when the PCs return to Gaer Dolbenmaen 
they will learn of the missing Traders, and Dingad can request the PCs investigate. 
52 This could be because he sympathises with their plight, he knows one of their number, or he feels that the Outlaws 
are operating in Arfon or Afflogion territory. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
85 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O02. Banditry 
Procedural Goal 
Lead the PCs to the Point of No Return, the Afanc attack at Llyn Cwmystradllyn (O04). 
Emotional Goal 
Introduce the PCs to the plight of those outside the law. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: Dunoding, Arfon and Afflogion triple Border, in the vicinity of the L3.9 Outlaw camp. 
An 8-day ago53 three Saxon Traders and their laden donkey left L5.6 Gaer Dolbenmaen promising to return 
in four days. They had stated that they intended to travel west to L1.3 Garn Boduan to trade some fine 
weapons with Einion’s Ambaxtos Warriors, but they have not been seen since. Anyone interviewed on the 
route will proffer their opinion that this may be down to the Outlaws who have been robbing travellers on 
the route recently. The Outlaws are known to operate out of dense forest to the north of the main route 
down to Garn Boduan.  
If the PCs search out the Outlaws, then eventually they will come across the group. There should be the 
number of PCs +2 (Use typical Outlaw stats from Men and Women, (pg. 144), but -1 to their Strength and 
Agility). Use Scouting rules from the Encounters section, FLGG pg 146, to determine if the PCs notice the 
threat in time to act on it. 
The Outlaws, a ragtag band of skinny, unwashed, and poorly clothed Britons, who look like they have not 
eaten in days, are led by Morvyn Rustyhand(C3.11). The Outlaws avoid killing and maiming, when possible 
(as much to avoid the attention of the Rhi as anything else), although womenfolk will naturally expect to 
be molested. The band is very unlikely to attack a well-armed group such as the PCs, but they will defend 
themselves if provoked. The band, or whomever is left alive if the PCs attack them, will deny seeing any of 
the Saxon Traders and there will be no sign of the Traders’ goods in the Outlaw’s mean little camp, deep 
in the forest. The Outlaws will claim, however, that one of their scouts, a snotty-nosed teen called Gall, saw 
the Saxon Traders approach L5.7 Llyn Cwmystradllyn, but that “something horrible” got them.  
The Blight has led to starvation and conflict within Gwynedd and as a result the number of people falling 
foul of the law and being Outlawed has spiked. Morvyn’s group is just one of several that now must find 
ways to stay alive outside of the protection of a Rhi.  The Outlaws are very obviously starving and little 
threat to the PCs. They will present pitiful stories of how they came to be outlawed (e.g. mistaken identity, 
stealing food to live, dispute with neighbours, etc) and will accept any food with obvious gratitude. 
Dilemma 
The PCs are operating on behalf of the Rhi, and, by rights, they should pass sentence and kill the outlaws. 
They need to decide whether to remove the Outlaws or leave them be.  
 
53 One insular Celtic week, “wythnos”. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
86 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Choices 
1. Kill or drive off the Outlaws, noting that Dingad’s advisor Casanauth wishes the PCs to remove 
the Outlaws. 
or 
2. Assist or leave the Outlaws in peace, noting that Dingad’s slave concubine Áine wants the Outlaws 
protected.  
Consequences 
1. Anyone killing Outlaws54 in full knowledge of their plight, and who doesn’t have the Cold-Blooded 
talent, will take 1 point damage to Empathy. Dingad’s slave concubine Áine will oppose the PCs 
politically after this point, but Casanauth, his chief advisor will look favourably on them. The PCs’ 
Reputation Score will increase by 1. 
2. Leaving the Outlaws at large means that the Outlaws will continue to harass this trade route. Also, 
if it becomes known that the PCs left the Outlaws in peace rather than enforcing the Rhi’s justice, 
then those recognising the PCs will act accordingly, including Áine and Casanauth55.  
Rooting Interest 
1. The PCs desire to keep their Reputation high with Dingad 
2. The PCs conflicted feelings about others in Dingad’s court, especially Áine and Casanauth. 
Next Steps 
The PCs should come away from the encounter with the knowledge that the Saxon Traders were last seen 
near L5.4 Braich y Gornel and L5.7 Llyn Cwmystradllyn. This should lead directly to O04. Afanc, (pg. 89). 
Court intrigue may continue, with Áine and Casanauth each either helping or hindering the PCs; both in 
court in O05. Censure (pg 92) and after they have left on their travels. 
 
 
54 or disarming them and leaving them to starve to death. 
55 I.e. Other Outlaws will be predisposed to looking favourably on the PCs, others, less favourably. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
87 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O03. Bwbachod Attacks 
Procedural Goal 
Lead the PCs to the Point of No Return, the Afanc attack at Llyn Cwmystradllyn (O04). 
Emotional Goal 
Show the PCs that Creatures of Magic are being affected also by The Blight. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L5.5 Ffridd Isaf ring cairn, Dunoding 
An 8-day ago three Saxon Traders and their laden donkey left L5.6 Gaer Dolbenmaen, promising to return 
in four days. Barra Gwallgof, the old mad hermit in nearby L5.4 Braich y Gornel, has claimed that he saw 
them travelling north into the mountains, but they have not been seen since. 
Anyone interviewed on the route will proffer their opinion that the traders likely fell afoul of the Bwbachod, 
who infest the hills and mountains north of Gaer Dolbenmaen. 
If the PCs follow paths into the mountains, then they will quickly pick up obvious signs of a Bwbachod 
raiding party, however the raiding party is obviously travelling south-east. No Survival roll is required to 
follow the Bwbachod; their trail is full of discarded camp detritus, the remains of small animals, the tramp 
of many small feet. The PCs should come across the Bwbachod in the fields above the Llyn as they attack 
the offerings and Christian Icons laid about the Ffridd Isaf ring cairn in broad daylight.  There should be 
the number of PCs x 3 Bwbachod. The PCs should be told that they understand these creatures do not 
operate willingly in the daytime. 
The Bwbachod are so absorbed in their destruction, that they have not arranged scouts and the PCs do not 
need to roll Scouting to see if they notice the little creatures before they run into them. 
The Bwbachod will be disadvantaged by the bright sunlight (reduce Base Dice on skill and weapon checks 
by 1D) and, if attacked in force by all the PCs, will concentrate their attacks on the most impressive looking 
PC in an attempt to use their numbers to best advantage. After two rounds the Bwbachod should have the 
measure of the PCs and if it looks unlikely that they will prevail, then they will attempt Retreat and Run 
actions. 
Dilemma  
The Bwbachod heavily outnumber the PCs, but the PCs have an advantage of surprise. If they attack the 
creatures though, the PCs may not learn anything useful.  
Choices 
1. Kill the Bwbachod whilst they are distracted, thus improving the PCs’ odds 
Or 
2. Talk to the creatures and try to see if they know what happened to the Saxon Traders. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
88 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Consequences 
If the PCs attack, then, presuming that at least some of the creatures get away, in the future the PCs will 
find these vindictive beings following the group when travelling in the mountains and waiting for an isolated 
spot for an ambush. 
If the PCs parley, then most of the Bwbachod will slowly back away and melt into any available under-
growth. However, their leader, Wraig Fawr(C5.8), and a retinue of 2 others will hold firm. The Bwbachod 
will indicate, if asked about the Traders, that they had seen Saxons by the lake, but that something other-
worldly “got them”. If asked why they are attacking the offerings, they will just say “taint, taint, taint!” 
Rooting Interest 
1. The Bwbachod are seen as dangerous and successfully driving them off will be well received in 
Dingad’s court. 
2. Bwbachod also known to have lots of gold coins and jewellery about their persons (1D6-4 Gold 
Coins each) 
3. Killing Bwbachod is unlikely to get the PCs the information they need about the Traders, although 
capturing and questioning one might give them the info they need. 
Next Steps 
If the PCs showed restraint, then they should come away from the encounter with the knowledge that the 
Saxon Traders were last seen near Braich y Gornel and Llyn Cwmystradllyn. This should lead directly to 
O04. Afanc.  
If the PCs attacked the Bwbachod, then the PCs will only be aware of the link with the Llyn if they capture 
and question a Bwbach. However, given the Llyn’s proximity to the ring cairn, then the events of O04 can 
be readily seen from the PCs vantage point, and they can choose to intervene. In this case, though, the 
PCs will arrive later, and the damage to the dam will be more severe and the number of slaves already taken 
by the Afanc should be higher.  
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
89 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O04. Afanc 
Procedural Goal 
The Point of No Return, after this the PCs’ relationship with Dingad will be affected and they should 
have increased impetus to deal with the The Blight. 
Emotional Goal 
Allow the PCs to make a difficult choice before they can prevail. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L5.7 Llyn Cwmystradllyn, Dunoding 
The Saxon Traders mentioned in Elno’s(C5.6) prophesy (O01. Augury on Moel Yscydion (pg 82)) had decided 
to visit Llyn Cwmystradllyn upon hearing of its Roman irrigation channels that are being used to create 
water meadows56. As they approached the waterside, an Afanc (Beings of Annwn, (pg. 146)) exploded out of 
the shallow water and the Traders and their donkey had been dragged below. The Traders were expected 
back in L5.6 Gaer Dolbenmaen no later than 4 days after they had left, and only recently has anyone begun 
to wonder what has happened to the Traders.  
On approach by the PCs: 
The GM should describe the Llyn as the PCs approach or as they see it during their chase of the Bwbachod. 
They will know that the Roman irrigation system, which feeds the water meadows below the Llyn, is 
hoped by those in the area to ensure that they will have good crops this year. The water flow has allowed 
the locals to plant whilst the spring frosts are still occurring and, if the rains do not come, will ensure that 
the crops can mature. In support of this the dam and irrigation sluices are being carefully maintained by 
slaves. 
Context: 
This is the Point of No Return because it and the events at Ffridd Isaf Ring Cairn (whether the PCs are 
involved or not, O03) will set Cadfan’s Christians against the PCs and crystalise opinion within the various 
social strata in Dunoding regarding the PCs. 
 
 
56 Continuous water flow keeps the frost off the early grass crop and fertilises the soil with new silt.  


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
90 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Dilemma  
Trade goods consistent with those expected of the missing Saxon Traders will be found in the mud beside 
the Llyn. Shepherds in the area report that sheep have been going missing. Caethion (slaves) are working 
to reinforce the dam at the southwest end of the lake. As the PCs approach, they see the slaves under attack 
by something under the water.  
As you approach the waterside you first see trampled in the mud the smashed remains of pottery bottles – very like 
the ones those Saxon Traders had been peddling – “full of the best Gaulish wine” they’d said. There is some-
thing not right with the water here – there is the smell of rot and a blackness to the lake that appears wrong.  
Suddenly you hear a shout from the dam, a Slave is pointing at the water where a trail of bubbles is snaking its way 
towards the slaves. There is a shuddering impact, and the slaves on the dam lurch sideways. There is a scream, and 
a large greenish appendage has grabbed one of the slaves and is dragging them under the water.  
The Afanc has attacked the Slaves, and they are cowering on the irrigation equipment – any time one tries 
to escape across the dam top, then they are pulled screaming into the water. The Afanc is continually 
striking the wooden reinforced dam-top and it is starting to give, with water pouring through gaps. 
Choices 
• 
If the PCs run to the assistance of the slaves, they find that the dam top is crumbling in several 
places due to the Afanc’s assault. There are two separated groups of slaves clinging to the dam top, 
unable to escape, whilst the Afanc picks them off from the water. It is concentrating its attention 
on them, and it will not be distracted by attacks from other quarters. 
• 
The PCs should be told that, should the dam fail or the sluices be opened, then the isolated dam 
tops occupied by themselves and the slaves would then be well away from the water. 
• 
The Afanc’s attacks are also damaging the Dam. Every round when the Afanc is attacking people 
on the Dam top after the attack begins (considering the time take to engage, if this follows from 
O03) roll 2D6; on a 1 or 2 the dam fails.  
• 
The effect of erosion from opening the sluice in the centre of the dam will also be enough to cause 
the dam to fail. 
• 
Either way, the dam failure drains most of the water from the Llyn and effectively moves the 
aquatic Afanc away from the slaves and PCs. 
So, the PCs can thwart the Afanc without defeating it by force of arms, but at the cost of breaching the 
dam and threatening the newly planted crops in the water-meadow below.  
Consequences 
If the PCs choose to sacrifice the dam to save the remaining slaves (or the dam fails due to the Afanc 
attacks), then the PCs will have the gratitude of the slaves’ owners and the slaves themselves, all from Gaer 
Dolbenmaen. However, the damage to the water meadows below will be extensive and the irreplaceable 
Roman irrigation system will be irreparably damaged. Dingad(C5.5) will be under pressure to censure the 
PCs for their actions and the farmers of Braich y Gornel will be ill disposed to the PCs. Of course, the 
Afanc may still be at-large in the Llyn. 
If the PCs ignore the dam and concentrate on defeating the Afanc, then most of the slaves will likely be 
killed by the monster (as it is concentrating on them, because they have been disturbing its territory). The 
slaves’ owners will not have justification to criticise the PCs, but they will likely hear grumbling in Gaer 
Dolbenmaen when they next visit.   


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
91 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Effect on Reputation: If the Afanc is defeated by the PCs OR if the Dam is breached, then the PCs’ 
reputation increases by one (maximum).  
Rooting Interest 
1. The PCs may know some of the slaves from Gaer Dolbenmaen 
2. The PCs may know farmers of the land irrigated by the Llyn 
Next Steps 
These events and that of O03 lead directly to O05. Censure.  If the PCs leave the site to come to the aid of 
the Slaves, then any Bwbach bodies will be carried off by their comrades. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
92 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O05. Censure 
Procedural Goal 
Provides the Sting in the Tail for the Point of No Return; however, the PCs can still affect the outcome. 
Emotional Goal 
Put the PCs on the back foot and give them a reason to fight back. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L5.6 Gaer Dolbenmaen, Dunoding 
Tydecho(C3.14), a colleague of Abad Cadfan(C3.4)  has been attending to Riein (Queen) Ystradwel(C5.7), as he 
is trying to convert her to Christianity. She has expressed interest in his religion, but has not decided either 
way, yet. Unfortunately for the PCs Tydecho is in Gaer Dolbenmaen when word comes of the desecration 
of the Ffridd Isaf Ring Cairn (O03) by Christians who were travelling there to make more offerings. Ty-
decho was afterwards visited by a spirit who told him that the PCs were to blame. When news of the events 
at the Llyn broke (O04) this premonition was justified in his mind.  
After the events during O03 and O04, Dingad(C5.5) will be forced to deal with two unpleasant develop-
ments.  
1. The Christian offerings at the Ffridd Isaf ring cairn have been desecrated. Abad Cadfan’s envoy, 
Tydecho, will make representations to the Rhi demanding the culprits be delivered to Saint 
Cynog’s to face justice, and, worst of all, Dingad had given his oath to the Cadfan allowing the 
placement of offerings in a political gesture of goodwill. 
2. The Llyn Cwmystradllyn Dam and Irrigation system has either been destroyed or at least badly 
damaged. This will directly impact on the health of Dunoding Cantref and subsequently on the 
taxes he will receive. 
The PCs on the other hand, appear to be implicated in both events: 
1. The PCs were present at the Llyn, and they may have acted to destroy the dam, were unable to 
prevent its destruction, or were unable to prevent its damage.  
a. If the PCs point out they were saving lives, it will be noted that Slaves’ lives are less im-
portant than those of free Britons. 
2. Tydecho blames the PCs, and they were in the area at the time that desecration of the Ffridd Isaf 
ring cairn offerings was found. They may be claiming that Bwbachod were to blame, but their story 
of the goblins attacking the offerings is dubious for several reasons: 
a. The Bwbachod have never been known to do this before,  
b. They are known to be nocturnal.  
c. Any Bwbach body-part trophies taken by the PCs will have degraded to Agardite, and 
Elno(C5.6) may have told Dingad that the PCs came across Agardite on Moel Yscydion in 
O01.  
Location 
This will all likely play out in Gaer Dolbenmaen. If the PCs do not travel there directly after the events in 
O04, then Dingad(C5.5) will send his warlord Alcwn(C5.3) to fetch the PCs. If the PCs delay or refuse, then 
they may find that Dingad decides on guilt in their absence. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
93 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Dilemma  
Tydecho believes that it is the PCs who destroyed the pagan offerings at Ffridd Isaf Ring Cairn, and he 
demands that Dingad the Rhi punish the PC severely. Quote “The one true God is one of Love, but sometimes 
stern love is necessary”. As there are none of the Nine Witnesses to the supposed infraction57 , and as there are 
insufficient other witnesses58, then the Rhi decides to make his ruling based on the evidence presented.  
Dingad notes that his laws have not been breached, but if PCs have done as Tydecho charges, then the PCs 
will at the very least lose Dingad’s patronage, if not worse.  
Once all sides have presented their case, Dingad will make a judgement. Use this as a PC Manipulation vs 
Dingad Insight test, with the following modifiers. 
Add to the PCs’ Manipulation roll: 
+1D If the PCs destroyed the Afanc. 
+1D If the PCs point out the presence of small tracks around the ring cairn. 
+2D If the PCs can provide other proof of Bwbachod activity at the Ring Cairn, besides showing 
Dingad Agardite crystal remains59. 
Add to Dingad’s Insight roll: 
+1D if the PCs insist that Bwbachod were to blame for the desecration (as this is patently ridicu-
lous). 
+1D if Slaves were killed. 
+1D if the Dam was damaged, but not destroyed. 
+3D If the Dam was destroyed. 
Manipulation vs Insight test 
Outcome 
PCs have 3+ successes than 
Dingad 
Dingad supports the PCs and rejects Tydecho’s claims publicly. Din-
gad remains the PC’s patron, but the Arfon Christians are now openly 
hostile to Dunoding Cantref. 
PCs have 1 or 2 more successes 
than Dingad 
Dingad supports the PCs but feels he must placate Tydecho. He reluc-
tantly tells the PCs that he can no longer publicly support them, but 
that they still have his private support. 
Tie or Dingad has 1 more suc-
cess than the PCs 
Dingad supports Tydecho but rejects Outlawing them. He tells the 
PCs that he can no longer support them and relinquishes his patron-
age. 
Dingad has 2+ successes more 
than the PCs 
Dingad supports Tydecho and declares the PCs Outlawed in Du-
noding. 
Note: Attempts to convince Tydecho to drop the case will be difficult, given that he has Rank 2 in Incor-
ruptible; however, it should not be impossible. Tydecho does not care about the Afanc, the Slaves, or the 
Dam, so use the remaining modifiers above (i.e. the PCs can improve their roll by providing proof and 
Tydecho can improve his roll if the PCs insist that Bwbachod were to blame).  
 
57 The Naw Tafiodiog, or Nine Tongued ones, from “Letters on Welsh History” by John Samuel, 1852. See The Law (pg. 
150). 
58 12 needed under the Wager of Law (Wikipedia). 
59 Elno can attest that the PCs found Agardite at Moel Yscydion after all. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
94 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Choices 
The main choices open to the PCs here surround how to collect evidence in their favour, whether to suborn 
witnesses, and whether to appeal directly to Tydecho to drop the case,   
Consequences 
Casanauth(C5.4) as the highest ranking Uchelwyr noble will be responsible for carrying out any sentences. 
1. If the PCs lose Dingad’s(C5.5) Patronage (most likely) then they can operate freely in Dunoding, 
but they will no longer be able to carry out tasks in return for silver and cannot freely use the 
Functions of Gaer Dolbenmaen. 
2. If the PCs retain Dingad’s private support, then he will not support them in future, but they still 
can use the facilities of Gaer Dolbenmaen. 
3. If the PCs are Outlawed, then, in theory, their lives are forfeit in Dunoding Cantref to anyone who 
cares to kill them. In practice if they keep to themselves, or better yet, stay out of the Cantref, then 
it is unlikely that they will come to harm. 
4. If Dingad rules in the PCs’ favour, then the Arfon Christians will begin to lobby other Rhiau to 
act against Dunoding. This will erode Dingad’s position with Cadwallon and Einion at the very 
least and will change the political map for the remainder of the campaign. 
Rooting Interest 
• 
Self-preservation 
• 
Concern for other residents 
Next Steps 
At the time of this Obstacle the PCs should all experience the last of The Dreams. This should lead them 
towards finding Bodhmall in O06. 
 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
95 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O06. Finding Bodhmall 
Procedural Goal 
Give the PCs a path to resolution for the Blight. 
Emotional Goal 
Finding allies, when it seems that all around they have enemies. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.6 Dinas Affaraon, Arfon 
The PCs have learned that the Irish Druid Bodhmall(C3.3) (BAWTH-mall, hard “th” as in the) may know 
what is causing the Blight, but basic inquiries will reveal that outsiders find it difficult to be granted access 
to Dinas Affaraon, where she is known to reside. 
Dinas Affaraon lies to the north of Dunoding, in the passes through the Eryri Mountains. Travel there 
from Gaer Dolbenmaen involves 2 Open Terrain Hexes (6 Hex/Quarter day) and 3 Difficult Terrain Hexes 
(3 Hex/Quarter day) and can be accomplished in an easy 2 quarter-day’s travel.  
The hillfort, once the domain the High King of Britain, Vortigern, is now the administrative centre for the 
Druids of Gwynedd. Bodhmall, the Arch Druid rules here with a small group of Druids and Servants. There 
is magical protections on Dinas Affaraon to keep the Druids’ privacy. It is expected that only those knowing 
the way of the pagan gods and having the courage to confront an avatar of a god, will be able to breech the 
protection.  
The avatar is a Great Stag, an embodiment of Cernunnos, which brings down a mist onto the hilltop and 
misdirects all those who climb through the mist looking for the entrance. Only once the Stag is placated 
(or killed) will the mist dissipate and the geas be removed. 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
96 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
On travelling to Dinas Affaraon the PCs arrive to find the hilltop becoming enshrouded in an ever-deep-
ening fog. Their last view before the mist blocks all sight is of a path (1) that switchbacks up the steep 
hillside towards the hillfort’s entrance (2).  
Once the PCs start climbing the hill, whether on the path or by another means, they find they seem to keep 
looping back on themselves. They will repeatedly find themselves back beside the same rock.  
At this point they will see a vague figure off the path and above them. If they investigate, they find a great 
stag is barring their way. This stag is an avatar of Cernunnos and resembles the stag the PCs have seen in 
their dreams (The Dreams (pg. 78)). 
If they seek to retreat, the path takes them off the hill and the mist dissipates.
