The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
97 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Choices 
Any of the following actions will cause the mist to lift and the path resume its course to the entrance of 
Dinas Affaraon. 
1. Killing the stag60  
2. Provide a suitable offering to the stag, (successful Lore check to give hint) 
3. Dispel the mist with Weathermaster(3)61 
Offerings appropriate for Cernunnos include: wine, water, milk; antler sheds, leaves, soil, his sacred 
plants; acts of sexuality, primal drumming music, and entering an otherworldly state. 
Note: Dispel Magic causes the mist to lift momentarily and then it returns. Similarly, True Sight allows one 
to see the path, but the sight fades before one can traverse the path. A druid taking animal form could fly 
up over the mist and down to Dinas Affaraon, but they would be unable to return. 
Consequences 
1. If the stag is killed, Bodhmall will still meet with the PCs and provide information on ways to end 
The Blight (pg. 32), but the Druid faction will provide no other aid 
2. If the stag accepts an offering, then Bodhmall will meet the PCs and offer aid (see below for details) 
3. If the PCs dispel the mist or bypass it using Weathermaster or some other magical means, then 
Bodhmall will meet the PCs and agree to offer aid if requested with a successful Manipulation vs 
Insight test (see below for details) 
Whatever the outcome, Bodhmall will limit direct aid (see The Blight (pg. 32)) to lending the PCs the services 
of Angharad Twinspike(C3.1), should they require martial assistance. 
Rooting Interest 
1. A desire to understand the Blight 
2. A need for shelter and food after their journey 
3. Druids will have concerns about following their tenets 
Next Steps  
Bodhmall will carry out her augury in two steps. Rather than using ritual sacrifice and reading of entrails, 
she will use Neldoracht, the reading of clouds.  
1. This involves stripping naked, drinking milk from a horn, and then walking into the waters of the 
sacred pool (6), where the druids Windwhisperer and Maeldaf ritually cleanse her with salt, whilst 
Anghard Twinspike(C3.1) watches on disapprovingly.  
2. She then floats in the pool and stares at the sky overhead. After some time, she will come to herself 
and be helped out of the pool and re-robed by her attendants in white linen.  
She will tell the PCs that they must choose between the Way of Blood, the Way of Magic, and the Way 
of Spirit, but she will warn the PCs that none of these paths will be what they seem. If they seek further 
information, she will state: 
• 
“The Way of Blood will force you to deal with the Deceit of the Children of Man” 
 
60 Use Deer stats from FLGG pg 126 but increase Strength to 6 and Horn damage to 2. 
61 Requires Power Level 3, and therefore likely the help of an NPC. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
98 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
• 
“The Way of Magic will force you to deal with the Power of the Land” 
• 
“The Way of Spirit will force you to deal with the Mists of Annwn” 
Once they have chosen, then Bodhmall will continue the augury.  
1. This time she will be led down out of Dinas Affaraon to a forest glade, where a single white bull is 
tied to a huge oak tree.  
2. Bodhmall will cut the throat of the bull with a small knife, and as it topples over with a bellow and 
her white robes are sprayed with the lifeblood of the beast, she swiftly begins to cut into its body, 
pulling out entrails and examining them minutely with her fingertips.  
3. As the weak movements of the bull finally subside, she will turn to the PCs with their answer.  
When she does so, the weather changes abruptly, with black clouds gathering before quickly dispersing. A 
flock of sparrows takes flight, first circling the group sinistra (to the left) and then reversing to circle destra 
(to the right). Amongst this confusing state of omens, she provides clarification on their choice.  
If they choose: 
• 
The Way of Blood, then they learn they must travel to L4.6 Sancta Arcis and seek the Sorcerer 
who resides there. They should state their goal and ask them “What act of man or woman will restore 
the balance?” 
• 
The Way of Magic, then they learn they must travel to L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery and find 
one of the Blood of Cunnedda. They should state their goal and ask them “What power of the land 
can be restored and what cannot?” 
• 
The Way of Spirit, then they learn they must travel to L2.3 Caer Wydyr and seek a woman of 
Power who resides there. They should state their goal and ask them “What circle of power was hidden, 
but now can return to the source?”  
Bodhmall will state she feels she cannot provide any more information, such as where these places can be 
found, without risking making things worse (see The Blight (pg.32)). However, in recompense she will offer 
the PCs the services of Angharad Twinspike(C3.1), should they require martial assistance.  
A successful Lore check will reveal the relevant location of the places they seek, or the PCs can consult 
with other experts, such as Elno(C5.6). 
Mini Obstacle – as they leave Dinas Affaraon the PCs are pursued by beings of Annwn.  
• 
If they chose Blood, they are followed by a Brenin Llwyd.  
• 
If they chose Magic, they are followed by a pack of Ellyllon.  
• 
If they chose Spirit, they are followed by a Gwyllion.  
The encounters should be more unnerving than dangerous, and the Beings of Annwn should soon losing 
interest in the PCs after the initial skirmishing. 
Summary 
1. The Way of Blood leads to of Tobrecan(C4.4) the Sorcerer, who will help the PCs drive out the 
Christians. However, his real plans are to cause the downfall of Cadwallon, using his son Maelgwn 
as a pressure point.  
2. The Way of Magic leads to Maelgwn(C3.10), and then his friend Emrys(C6.2) (Myrddin the Arch-
druid in disguise) – Emrys is sympathetic to the PCs cause and leads them to the information of 
where the Eaglestone Amulet may be found (in a Dragon’s lair on L3.4 Carnedd y Cawr) 
3. The Way of Spirit leads to Morgen(C2.4), who will guide the PCs to retrieve Cernunnos’ Torc from 
Annwn, but after they retrieve it, instead of helping them return it to Cernunnos, she wants to use 
it to barter with the god Andraste for the return of her brother Mordred from Annwn. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
99 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O07. To Ynys Mons 
Procedural Goal 
Deliver the PCs to Sancta Arcis. 
Emotional Goal 
Demonstrate political differences at play in Arfon. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: Ynys Mons, including L4.1 Aberffraw 
The PCs are travelling to L4.6 Sancta Arcis, at the far end of the island of Ynys Mons in Arfon. Travelling 
there they will start to understand that although Arfon is the most overtly powerful of the Cantrefs in 
Gwynedd, that there are political divides and Cadwallon is weaker than generally understood.  
Note: See the Timeline & Faction Map (pg. 80), if Cadwallon and Tobrecan are in open conflict at this point 
(possible with T6 and T9), then this will significantly hinder access and egress from Ynys Mons. 
The Lyblæca Tobrecan rules the island as an Uchelwr to Cadwallon and pays tax render, but his power is 
growing and soon he will look to challenge the Rhi for power in Arfon. Meanwhile, the local druids have 
retreated to their grove, L4.3 Llwyn o Gerrig, after Tobrecan cowed the peaople with arcane power displays.  
In whatever manner the PCs travel to the straights between Ynys Mons and the mainland, when they look 
for means to travel to the island, they will find that the local boatmen strangely reluctant to take them. They 
will ask the exorbitant price of 5 silver per passenger for the journey. Further, the PCs will be told that they 
only will land at one spot, L4.1 Aberffraw on the southwest coast, and that they will not sell or rent their 
vessels to the PCs. The Uchelwr of the island has recently decreed that only landings may happen there, 
and those who have defied this injunction suffered mysterious demises.  
Of course, the PCs can steal a boat and make their own way there, however, locals will be quick to point 
out in discussions with the PCs that the currents in the straight are treacherous due to the Cerevus whirl-
pools62.  
If the PCs land at Abberfraw they will be greeted by suspicious Ambaxtos, who are posted here to intercept 
travellers on behalf of Tobrecan(C4.4)  (Gofan ap Gareth(C4.1) leading (PCs x 3) Ambaxtos). If the PCs 
manage to land elsewhere, then Tobrecan’s network of informers in the Trefs will quickly inform him of 
any strangers who openly travel on the island and Gofan and his Ambaxtos will be sent to intercept the 
PCs.  
On meeting with Tobrecan’s Ambaxtos the PCs will initially be taken for rustlers or reivers if there are no 
druids with party, or they will be taken as druid emissaries if there are druids. If the latter, then they are 
escorted to the nearby grove of L4.3 Llwyn o Gerrig, but not allowed elsewhere. If the former, then the 
Ambaxtos will look to drive the PCs away. The Ambaxtos fear Tobrecan more than druids and their leader 
Gofan bears an artefact, a Sonio Grwyn, to call for his assistance.  
The druids in Llwyn o Gerrig will welcome visitors but be unwilling to leave the grove or act openly against 
Tobrecan at this time. 
Note: 
 
62 One of Nennius’ Wonders of the Isle of Anglesey, now known as the Swellies in English or Pwll Ceris in Welsh. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
100 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Locals can take the PCs across in vessel safely to Abberfraw. If the PCs choose to navigate them-
selves across the straights at L4.2 Pwll Cerist after acquiring a vessel, then: 
Use the Sea Travel system with the following difficulty adjustors for the Survival Roll: 
 
Slack -2D / Flood or Ebb -4D 
 
If at Night -1D 
They PCs may choose to travel at slack tide (if they think if it and if they have the time63,). Other-
wise roll a D6, on a 1 or 6 the tide is Slack, otherwise it is flooding or ebbing.  
On a Survival Roll Failure use the Sea Travel mishap table on FLPG pg. 156 and adjust as necessary 
for the circumstances of the voyage. 
Artefacts: Gofan ap Gareth holds a Sonio Grwn rattle (A15). 
Dilemma 
The PCs will likely be faced with a contingent of Ambaxtos warriors who, at best, will agree to escort them 
between the druid grove at L4.3 Llwyn o Gerrig and L4.1 Abberfraw and back again. At worst the Ambax-
tos will look to drive the PCs out of Ynys Mons entirely. As the PCs are either looking to travel to Sancta 
Arcis, if following the Way of Blood or the Way of Magic, or Bryn Celli Ddu, if following the Way of 
Spirit, then they will need to find a way to break the deadlock. 
Choices 
The PCs can choose to negotiate, to cow or intimidate, or to steal away from the Ambaxtos. Given the fear 
that the Ambaxtos hold towards Tobrecan any Manipulation roll will be at -3D. 
Consequences 
If the PCs attack the Ambaxtos or break an Ambaxtos (say using the Sharp Tongue Talent) then reduce 
their Manipulation and related rolls when dealing with Tobrecan by a number of dice appropriate for the 
actions of the PCs (from -2D for the PCs evading the Ambaxtos, to -4D+ if the PCs kill an Ambaxtos). 
Rooting Interest 
Getting to their desired destination without upsetting the local ruler would appear to be obviously a good 
course of action. 
Next Steps 
If following the Way of Blood, this should lead directly to the PCs meeting with Tobrecan, either in Sancta 
Arcis or elsewhere, with O10. An Audience at Sancta Arcis (pg 104). 
If following the Way of Magic, then depending on the Obstacle path taken, see Obstacle Map (pg. 79), this 
leads either to meeting Tobrecan with O10. An Audience at Sancta Arcis (pg 104), or to the PCs travelling to 
Bryn Ceilli Ddu for the climax with the dragons in O23. Dracones Venire (pg 142). 
If following the Way of Spirit, this should allow the PCs to approach Bryn Celli Ddu, carrying Cernunnos’ 
torc, with O21. Interception (pg 138). 
 
63 Slack is 1 hour each side of high or low tide, which occur roughly 6 hours apart. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
101 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O08. To St Cynog’s 
Procedural Goal 
Deliver the PCs to St Cynogs. 
Emotional Goal 
Show the PCs the influence that the Christians have on Arfon. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: Arfon, including L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery 
As the PCs travel north through Arfon Cantref they see traditional pagan sites of worship and veneration 
now converted to places of Christian offering. There are also few signs of Druids and a reduced deference 
to them here.  
The PCs will at some point come across a group of monks from St Cynog’s who are helping Eillt farmers 
rebuild their roundhouse and restore their farm. The farm suffered collateral damage when a drunk giant 
made its way through, heading towards nearby hills. Any Druids are treated with suspicion by these monks. 
The PCs will notice that one young boy of about 10 years has been separated from his family and is being 
taken towards St Cynogs by an older monk leading a donkey. 
Dilemma 
It will quickly become clear to the PCs that the child being led away is being taken to start a new life as an 
ascetic. This is the implicit cost of the aid provided by the St Cynog’s monks. 
Choices 
• 
If the PCs interfere with the child being taken, whose name is Buan, then the monks will down 
tools and return to St Cynogs, leaving the family to starve. 
• 
If the PCs decide to help the Eillt themselves, then their mission is put on hold and The Blight will 
continue to ravage the lives of the locals. 
• 
If the PCs leave the situation as is, then the Eillt family will lose their child forever to the Christians. 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs prevent the child being taken by the monks, then future interactions with St Cynog’s 
will be at a disadvantage. Reduce Manipulation and related rolls by a number of dice appropriate 
for the actions of the PCs (from -2D for the PCs convincing the family to demand their child back, 
to -6D+ if the PCs kill a monk). 
• 
If the PCs allow the child to be taken, then if visiting St Cynog’s they will see young Buan has 
become a close personal attendant to Abad Cadfan or one of the other senior leaders. The leader 
will appear to be very preoccupied with their new assistant. 
Rooting Interest 
Conflict between the PCs’ desire to rid the land of The Blight and possible concerns over what will become 
of Buan at St Cynogs. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
102 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Next Steps 
This should lead directly to the PCs finally arriving at St Cynog’s with 011. An audience at St Cynog’s (pg 101). 
O09. The Ferryman 
Procedural Goal 
Deliver the PCs to Ynys Enlli. 
Emotional Goal 
Allow the PCs to sacrifice something to the cause. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L1.1 Aberdaron, Afflogion 
The PCs will be travelling to Ynys Enlli, but to get there they must traverse the dangerous waters between 
it and the mainland. Local fishermen in Aberdaron will outright refuse to land on the island nor let their 
valuable boat be risked for the journey in the hands of the PCs, so their obvious choice is either to employ 
Barinthus the Ferryman(C1.2) or steal a boat and brave the waters.   
Barinthus is known to all the inhabitants of Aberdaron, and any local can direct the PCs to his hut, which 
sits just above the high tide line by the mouth of Afon Daron.  There is an array of local fishermen’s boats 
also pulled up above the high tide line. The boats are simple, rowed, sewn plank types, like Bronze Age 
Ferriby boats. 
Dilemma 
Barinthus knows Morgen(C2.4) and has been instructed to only take visitors who are worthy to visit her. He 
will demand each passenger provide one of the following: 
• 
Reveal their Pride and leave it behind – The PC must erase their Pride and create a new Pride after 
they return from Ynys Enlli (only one PC may do this) 
• 
An Act of Service – The PC must carry out a task for Morgen. One per PC, which can include  
o Retrieving a ball of Mistletoe from L1.2 Ffynhonnell Aberdaron (which will require pla-
cating the Druids that protect it),  
o Travelling to L1.3 Garn Boduan and dropping a stone inscribed with Morgen’s name in 
Ogham into their well,  
o Finding Mellus the Medic, Fergus sil Coumcille, or some other local specified by Barinthus, 
and carrying out whatever service they require. This service could include: gathering special 
herbs, taking a calf to market, finding the outlaws and delivering a message, etc. 
Choices 
Either carry out Barinthus’ tasks or try to make their own way to the island. If the PCs steal a boat, then 
they will need to do so undetected, likely meaning taking the boat at night and crossing the perilous sound 
at night.

If the PCs choose to navigate themselves, then use the Sea Travel system with the following difficulty

They PCs may choose to travel at slack tide (if they think if it and if they have the time64,). Otherwise roll a

The PCs need to get to the island if they are to progress with their various quests.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
104 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O10. An Audience at Sancta Arcis 
Procedural Goal 
Introduce the PCs to Tobrecan. 
Emotional Goal 
Give the PCs the encouragement that they have a concrete strategy to combat The Blight. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L4.6 Sancta Arcis, Ynys Mons 
Tobrecan(C4.4) is based in Sancta Arcis, a square three-walled65, former Roman fort on a small island at the 
northwest tip of the isle of Ynys Mons. 
Note: See the Timeline & Faction Map (pg. 80), if Cadwallon(C3.5) has sent his advisor Balthildis(C3.2) to 
negotiate with Tobrecan before this time (T6), then she will be present in the fortress and it will be in a 
state of military readiness. 
When approached by the PCs Tobrecan will be suspicious of any druids in the party, although he will be 
interested in any news from Dunoding and Afflogion. The GM should adjust Tobrecan’s attitude based 
upon the circumstances of their meeting; the PCs may be brought as captives, victors over his forces, or 
they may have manipulated his Ambaxtos into escorting them to his stronghold.  
Once Tobrecan learns that the PCs are approaching him for help in overcoming The Blight, and once he 
is convinced that the PCs will be useful to him, then he will flip to being overly friendly and helpful.  
However, Scáthach(C4.3), his warlord may not be so inclined. She is fond of her lieutenant, Gofan, and 
unless the PCs have left on good terms with the man, then she will be out to make trouble for the PCs.  
Tobrecan will ask to discuss the PC’s mission with them the next day, as he means to investigate his library 
before suggesting a course of action. In the meantime, a feast is to be held. During the feast Scáthach will 
contrive to create an altercation with one of the PCs if she is not on good terms with them. This could be 
simply by bumping into them and attempting to incite them to act rashly, or by deliberately taking a state-
ment by a PC out of context and beginning an argument.  
Artefacts: Tobrecan hold the sword Bitor (A01) and the Codex Climaci Rescriptus (A03). Scáthach holds a 
Sonio Grwn rattle (A15). 
 
 
 
65 Where the fourth wall would be is a low cliff overlooking the sea. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
105 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
However it occurs, Scáthach will deliberately incite an argument during the feast with a PC and then turn 
to her lord and demand to be allowed to take satisfaction. Scáthach wants a duel.   
Choices 
• 
The PCs can try and win over Scáthach, but this should only be possible if the PCs know enough 
to address the root problem – that Scáthach believes that the PCs mistreated her lieutenant. If they 
can address that issue, then a Manipulation vs Insight roll can be made with relevant modifiers 
listed in O07. To Ynys Mons (pg. 100) 
• 
The PCs can accept one-on-one combat between Scáthach and their chosen champion, presumably 
their most capable warrior. This will take place by the fire in the feasting chamber.  
• 
The PCs can reject the offer of the duel, and Scáthach will stand down.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
106 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs win over Scáthach, then there are no long-term consequences. 
• 
If the PCs accept the duel and Scáthach is maimed or killed, then Tobrecan will not take kindly to 
losing his trusted vassal. All subsequent relevant rolls when the PCs are dealing with Tobrecan 
should be at -2D 
• 
If the PCs reject the duel, then Tobrecan will lose some respect for the PCs.  All subsequent rele-
vant rolls when the PCs are dealing with Tobrecan should be at -1D. 
Note: none of these consequences will prevent Tobrecan from providing the information listed in Next 
Steps, as he believes he is using the PCs to achieve his own goals by misdirecting them.  
Rooting Interest 
The PCs still want something from Tobrecan, so they will need to consider their actions carefully. 
Next Steps 
The day after the feast Tobrecan will meet with the PCs and tell them that he has consulted his best sources 
of information. He will craft his answer depending on the PCs’ quest, although in each case he is being 
duplicitous with the PCs: 
If following the Way of Blood: After hearing the question “What act of man or woman will restore the balance?”, 
Tobrecan will tell the PCs that recovering the Lancea Longini (aka the Spear of Destiny) is the key to over-
coming The Blight. He will tell the PCs that if this artefact is presented to the son of the Rhi Cadwallon, 
Maelgwn(C3.10), then he will use it to free Gwynedd of Christianity66. Tobrecan will tell the PCs that he 
believes the Lancea Longini (A08) was last held by an Aillt in L3.3 Caer Rhun of the lineage of Cof (See 
Sïþæbæd Straightback(C3.13)). Tobrecan knows that Maelgwn currently resides in L3.10 Saint Cynog’s 
Monastery. This leads to O13. Caer Rhun Search (pg 114). 
If following the Way of Magic: After hearing the question along the lines of “what must we do with the 
Eaglestone Amulet to stop The Blight”, Tobrecan will tell the PCs that off the northeast tip of Ynys Mons lies a 
small island, L4.7 Ynys Lannog. On this island lives a hermit who he believes keeps a rare Codex (A04) 
which explains the use of Eaglestones. Tobrecan will state, “Perhaps the Codex will explain how they may be used 
to stop The Blight?” 67. This leads to O20. Illtud The Hermit (pg 133). 
If following the Way of Spirit: Once Tobrecan learns that the PCs bear Cernunnos’ Torc (A02), then, 
after consulting various strange tomes, he will tell them that the Torc must be taken to the nearby L4.2 
Bryn Ceilli Ddu, where it must be destroyed to release the old gods power to the wellspring of Annwn68. 
He will also tell them that Morgen seeks the torc for her own purposes. He will tell them she is obsessed 
with returning her brother Mordred from Annnwn – her judgement will be clouded if offered a way to get 
him back. As they arrive at Bryn Celli Ddu the PCs will be caught by Morgen in O22. Interception (pg 138). 
 
66 Tobrecan is being duplicitous here. He knows that should the PCs do this, then Maelgwn will be overtaken by the 
power of the artefact and will end up martyring the monks – increasing their power in Gwynedd and weakening his 
enemy, Maelgwn’s father Cadwallon. 
67 Tobrecan is being duplicitous here. He knows that should the PCs do this, then the information in the Codex may 
incite the PCs to rise against Cadwallon.  
68 Tobrecan is being duplicitous here. He knows that should the PCs do this, then Cernunnos power in the realm will 
be weakened further and the Christian’s power increased, to the detriment of his enemy Cadwallon.  


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
107 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O11. An Audience at St Cynog’s 
Procedural Goal 
Introduce the PCs to Maelgwn who can provide the next step in the quest. 
Emotional Goal 
Show the PCs the Christians’ powerbase in Gwynedd. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery, Arfon 
The PCs have arrived at the former hillfort of Caer Seion, now a monastery devoted to Saint Cynog. This 
converted iron age fortification looms over the Gwynedd coastline and looks out to the headland of Great 
Orme. The monastery is headed by Abott Cadfan(C3.4). Recently, they have welcomed Maelgwn(C3.10), the 
15-year-old son of Rhi Cadwallon, who has joined them to learn the ways of Christianity. The prince is 
sequestered in the strong high-tower of the monastery and is heavily guarded on rare visits outside the walls. 
The PCs will be looking to meet Maelgwn69. This is because they are searching out one of the blood of 
Cunnedda, if they are following The Way of Blood or The Way of Magic, or because the Giant Custennin 
has indicated that Maelgwyn knows what should be done with Cernunnos’ Torc, if they are following The 
Way of Spirit. 
However, Maelgwn is a prized and jealously guarded guest of the Monastery. Getting access to the prince-
ling will not be easy. 
Artefact: Cadfan holds Saint Cynog’s Gilded Armilla (A14) 
Details of Saint Cynog’s - The monastery sits on a narrow ridge about 250m above the sea below. A single 
rampart protects the outer enclosure, whilst a more complex arrangement covers the inner enclosure. Un-
usually the outer and inner enclosure each have different entrances and there is no obvious way of access 
between the two. The outer enclosure contains about 50 roundhouses, whilst the inner enclosure has about 
six, plus a large round tower at the western end of the promontory. The original hillfort of Caer Seion has 
been repurposed by Cadfan, so now the tower and inner enclosure houses the higher status monks, whilst 
the outer enclosure is reserved for lower status monks and visitors. 
 
 
69 Although they may not know it until they arrive and talk to residents and learn that a prince is living within the 
walls. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
108 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
Visitors are generally welcomed to St Cynogs70, but access to the inner enclosure is restricted to the faithful 
and anyone asking to see Prince Maelgwn will be refused outright.  
Choices 
• 
The PCs can try and Manipulate their way to being given an audience. It will take more a success 
on a Manipulation roll; however, the PCs must find an advantage somewhere and then hammer it 
until they succeed. (e.g. blackmailing a servant).  
• 
The PCs can try and force their way into the inner tower, but the monks have powerful protectors 
in the 10 Warrior Guards and The Grim, which will appear if monks are attacked (see  O21. The 
Grim (p.g 136)). 
• 
The PCs can try and use Magic to lure Maelgwn out (he is not restrained and may leave if he wishes) 
or to Manipulate a monk or servant to do similar. 
Consequences 
If the PCs force their way in or harm monks or their servants, then in the aftermath Cadfan will send 
messages to Cadwallon requesting the PCs be outlawed. How Cadwallon(C3.5) responds should reflect the 
PCs reputations, the supposed infractions, and whether the PCs make any attempts to defend themselves.  
 
70 Although if the PCs have attacked or been in conflict with the Monks in O08. To St Cynog’s, then adjust accordingly.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0

Rooting Interest 
By now the PCs likely will have their own axes to grind with the monks, so they may be incentivised to be 
heavier handed than they might otherwise be.  
On the other hand, even if the Players forget, the Characters will be very aware that open conflict with the 
monks of St Cynogs are a recipe for trouble down the road. 
Next Steps 
When the PCs name themselves or indicate the reason why they are seeking him, the Prince will gasp and 
exclaim “Emrys, he told me that you would seek me out!” 
If following the Way of Blood, the PCs will be trying to bring Maelgwn the Lancea Longini. This leads 
immediately to O19. Maelgwn’s Downfall (pg 132). 
If following the Way of Magic, the PCs will be trying to talk to Maelgwn in the hope that the reason for 
their quest will be revealed. After asking “What power of the land can be restored and what cannot?” Maelgwn will 
explain that his friend, Emrys Nofather(C6.2), had told him of the PCs quest and that if Maelgwn felt them 
worthy that he should give them his token, a silver coin with an “M” inscribed in Latin lettering, and send 
them to Emrys who can be found in the nearby mines on L6.1 Cyngreawdr Fynydd. This leads to O14. 
Finding Emrys (pg. 116). 
If following the Way of Spirit, the PCs will be 
trying to bring Cernunnos’ Torc to Maelgwn in 
the hope that he can tell them how to properly 
dispose of the torc. Maelgwn will explain that his 
friend, Emrys Nofather(C6.2), had told him of 
the PCs quest and that if Maelgwn felt them wor-
thy that he should tell them that the sacred object 
must be returned to Cernunnos by placing it in 
the well in the centre of L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu on 
Ynys Mons. This leads to O07. To Ynys Mons. (pg. 
99). He will also say that Emrys also told him that 
the witch Morgen is after the torc and that her 
weakness is that close contact with bronze ham-
pers her power. 
Note: Maelgwn will be unable to tell the PCs 
much detail of his new friend. He will say that he 
met first met Emrys whilst walking on the head-
land. Since then, Emrys has taken to visiting him 
on occasion in his high room in the tower, some-
how bypassing the guards who know nothing of 
his visits. However, since Imbolc Emrys has 
stopped his visits and Maelgwn is concerned at 
his friend’s absence. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
110 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O12. An Audience at Caer Wydyr 
Procedural Goal 
Introduce the PCs to Morgen and inform them that Tre’r Ceiri is the source of the information they need. 
Emotional Goal 
Give the PCs some cause to be suspicious of Morgen in their future dealings with her. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L2.3 Caer Wydyr, Ynys Enlli 
The PCs have arrived on the sacred Isle of Ynys Enlli, this small island is home to the fortress of Caer 
Wydyr, which is nestled on the lowlands beside the central rocky heights. Although there are places like 
Neithon’ cave, which can be found with a little searching, the island’s tiny population largely resides within 
Caer Wydyr. Morgen(C2.4) fearsome reputation will likely be known to the PCs and the GM can play into 
the eeriness felt as the ominous fortress is approached and scatter ill-omens, such as birds circling anti-
clockwise, and the like71. 
Artefacts: Morgen’s Scrying Bowl (A10) in her sanctum (6) 
The Caer Wyder complex consists of a round tower of about 16m diameter appended to a stone courtyard, 
the entrance to which is protected by an unusual Ghost Fence (1). The courtyard (2) contains Caer Wydyr’s 
fabled apple orchard, which grow under an array of huge pieces of plate glass. It is this impossible-to-make 
glass which gives the fortress its name. The orchard is watered by the nearby L2.2 Holy Well, via a lead 
gutter and a stone basin. 
The tower construction is like that of the contemporary Pictish Brochs. There is a staircase that circles 
upwards between inner and outer walls, and smoke escapes and light enters through gaps and chimneys in 
the inner wall. The roof is thatched with reeds from island marshland, and there is a thin parapet which 
runs around the un-crenelated top.   
 
 
71 See Omens and Oaths (pg 104) 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0

Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania)

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
112 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Dilemma 
As the PCs approach Caer Wydyr, a slight and wizened figure blocks their way. A hoary old crow sits on 
its shoulder. This is Neithon the Hermit(C2.1) and his pet crow Penance. Neithon asks the PCs what their 
business is “with the she-witch who resides within”. Whatever their answer he will try and drive the PCs away, 
apparently heedless to his own safety. Neithon has sworn to try to stymie Morgen(C2.4) at every opportunity, 
and the arrival of the PCs appears to be such a chance. If 
the PCs try to understand his reasons for driving them off 
or try to win him over, they will find he is not forthcoming 
and is very difficult to Manipulate72. Additionally, he will be 
suspicious of any subject that involves Morgen and any Ma-
nipulation roll by a PC that involves her will be a -4D. 
Background: Neithon is racked with guilt because he feels complicit in the ruin of the community on the 
island. Several years ago, Morgen arrived and decided to make the island her centre of power. Nearing the 
end of a year of poor harvest, she incited Neithon, their druid, to sacrifice the community’s livestock in a 
bid to appease the gods. Without grain nor livestock, many of the community starved to death during the 
subsequent harsh winter, and the few eillt left fled to L1.1 Aberdaron in the spring. In this vacuum Morgen 
took control of Ynys Enlli and Neithon renounced his vows and retreated to a little cave on the island to 
live as a hermit. Note: As Neithon has renounced druidism, he no longer can cast spells.  
Choices 
• 
The PCs can attempt to convince Neithon that their intentions are not to aid Morgen and that they 
are only seeking to end The Blight. 
• 
The PCs can physically restrain, attack, or disable Neithon in order to pass on. 
• 
The PCs can back off but try another way to reach Morgen. This could be by calling to her, magi-
cally or otherwise73, to come out of Caer Wydr, by taking on animal form and flying or sneaking 
into Caer Wydyr, or by simply trying to sneak past or otherwise distract Neithon. 
Consequences 
• 
In the unlikely event that the PCs placate Neithon, then he will cantankerously acquiesce after 
implicating Morgen, in very general terms, in the destruction of the previous community on the 
island. He will then return to his cave. However, Morgen will be aware of this conversation and 
any subsequent Manipulation attempts of her by the PCs will be a -2D due to her new suspicion 
of their motives. 
• 
If the PCs physically restrain, attack, or disable Neithon in order to pass on, then Morgen will be 
more obliging to the PCs (+2D on relevant rolls with her), but Neithon, if still alive, will become 
their sworn enemy. 
• 
If the PCs back off but find another way to reach Morgen, then the situation remains unchanged. 
Rooting Interest 
None 
 
72 With a basic 8D plus a D8 Artefact dice on his Insight roll to resist Manipulation due to his Skills and Talents.      
73 For example, by giving a message to Barita the pictish slave when she comes out of the fortress one day. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
113 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Next Steps 
The door to Caer Wydyr complex is cracked open, but there are two skulls with unnaturally long canines 
embedded in the door jamb, making a Ghost Fence74 (6D Fear Attack to cross), (1). If the PCs enter the 
complex, then they will be greeted by Barita a female pictish slave. She can take the PCs to a small glass 
topped courtyard filled with vibrant apple trees, (2). There they will be asked to wait whilst she gets her 
mistress.  
When Morgen(C2.4) shows up it is with her sisters, Morgause(C2.3) and Blasine(C2.2). She will try to make her 
entrance as impressive and as mysterious as possible. She will seek to use the PCs intentions to best ad-
vantage.  
• 
If following The Way of Blood, Morgen’s goal will be for the PCs to remedy to situation with 
Maelgwn and improve his reputation by blackening Cadfan’s. This is because she has great plans 
for the princeling. 
• 
If following The Way of Magic, Morgen’s goal will be for the PCs to disrupt the Dragon’s power 
in Gwynedd, as her enemy Emrys’ power is linked to that of the Dragans.   
• 
If following The Way of Spirit, Morgen’s goals will be for the PCs to recover Cernunnos’ Torc, 
but not to end The Blight, rather because she needs it for her own ends.  
Eventually Morgen will take the PCs up to her sanctum,(6), at the top of the tower behind the courtyard. 
The staircase rises within the thick walls, eventually opening out into a large room. Morgen will then listen 
to the PCs’ story and then banish them from the chamber. They will hear occasional screams and yelps, 
and deep guttural male voices, and then Morgen will emerge. She then will tell the PCs: 
• 
The Way of Blood. Morgen will tell the PCs that the spirits of Annwn have indicated that the 
High Druid of Britain, Oron(C1.4), knows a secret that will destabilise Abad Cadfan(C3.4)  and Saint 
Cynog’s Monastery. Unfortunately, Oron has been dead for centuries, his ghost though can be 
found at the abandoned hillfort of  L1.4 Tre’r Ceiri. Morgen will warn the PCs that Oron’s price 
will be steep, perhaps more than the PCs are willing to bear. 
• 
The Way of Magic; Morgen will pick up the Eaglestone Amulet (A06) and examine it. She will 
tell the PCs that the High Druid of Britain, Oron(C1.4), was the last one who knew how to harness 
its power. His ghost haunts nearby L1.4 Tre’r Ceiri, and she suggests that they travel there to 
consult with it. Morgen will warn the PCs that Oron’s price will be steep, perhaps more than the 
PCs are willing to bear. 
• 
The Way of Spirit; The PCs will have asked “What circle of power was hidden, but now can return to the 
source?” Morgen now tells them that the circle of power refers to Cernunnos’ Torc, which can be 
found in Caer Sidi. However, no one knows where Caer Sidi lies in the mortal realms. She will tell 
the PCs that there is one in these lands who is ancient enough that he might know how to find the 
place, but his price will be steep, perhaps more than the PCs are willing to bear. The one they seek 
is Oron(C1.4), the ghost of a High Druid of Britain, who haunts the abandoned Hill Fort of L1.4 
Tre’r Ceiri to the northeast of Garn Boduan on the coast. Finally, she will tell the PCs it is not clear 
at this time what must be done with Cernunnos’ Torc. Note: from this point onwards, Morgen will 
leave the island and begin to keep track of the PCs exploits. Once they travel to Ynys Mons, then 
she begins to work towards getting the Torc from the PCs (see O22. Interception, (pg 138)).  
 
74 See Omens and Oaths (pg. 109). 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
114 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O13. Caer Rhun Search 
Procedural Goal 
Find the Lancea Longini 
Emotional Goal 
None 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.3 Caer Rhun and Canovium, Arfon 
The small tref of Caer Rhun sits just north of the abandoned remains of the roman fort of Canovium.  The 
Lancea Longini (A08), pg. 162, has been hidden in an old Roman cistern in Canovium by Sïþæbæd (Seetha-
bad) Straightback(C3.13) a female Saxon Ċeorl, who lives in Caer Rhun and acts as the headwoman. Un-
known to Sïþæbæd the cistern has become infested with Llamhigyn Y Dwr water leapers, who with the 
coming of The Blight have been attracted to the magic of the artefact hidden there. 
Sïþæbæd’s husband, Arthlwys ab Cof, was a wealthy aillt farmer. After inheriting the Lancea Longini, a 
revered family heirloom, he had used it to drive off reivers from Rhos 5 years ago. The artefact, thirsty for 
blood, overcame Arthlwys and drove him to turn and kill his son. In his grief Arthlwys took his own life 
and Sïþæbæd, fearful of its power, hid the Lancea Longini away.    
Sïþæbæd, although the headwoman, now lives a relatively quiet life alone in a large roundhouse in the tref. 
Anyone arriving in the area and asking after the descendants of Cof will be directed to her. She will be 
justifiably wary of newcomers, especially heavily armed and armoured strangers, although any presence of 
a druid will go a long way to persuade her of legitimate intent. 
Artefacts: The Lancea Longini (A08) is the subject of the search, and the Roman Galea Helmet (A13) is 
hidden along with the Lancea Longini. 
Valuables: Also hidden with the Artefacts is a leather pouch with 100 silver coins. 
Dilemma 
Sïþæbæd will be horrified once she realises that the PCs are searching for the Lancea Longini and that they 
have associated it with her late-husband’s family. She will hide her feelings (Use an opposed Insight (the 
PCs) vs Manipulation (Sïþæbæd) roll to see if they intuit her distress) and will initially deny knowledge of 
the artefact that the PCs seek. She will claim that she has work to do and politely but firmly ask the PCs to 
leave. 
Choices 
• 
The PCs could investigate further by interrogating Sïþæbæd’s neighbours. 
• 
The PCs could attempt to force Sïþæbæd to tell them what she knows. 
• 
The PCs could use magic, such as Visions of the Past, Intuition, or Speak to the Dead. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
115 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Consequences 
The PCs should be able to find out with some simple investigation that Sïþæbæd knows more than she is 
telling. The main consequence here will come from what they do with this information.  
• 
If dealt with sensitively then they should find out both where the artefact is hidden, but also 
Sïþæbæd will ensure that they know that it may be cursed.  
• 
On the other hand, if they are excessive and overbearing or overly forceful, then Sïþæbæd will 
reveal only that she has hidden the Lancea Longini in a cistern under the nearby abandoned fort 
of Canovium. 
Rooting Interest 
None, beyond needing to find the Lancea Longini. 
Next Steps 
The abandoned Roman fortification of L3.3 Caer Rhun and Canovium can provide an extensive Adventure 
Site, or it can be a quick stop on the path for the PCs. The cistern is still water filled, and the PCs may need 
to carry out some work to drain it sufficiently to access the depths where the Lancea Longini rests – this 
could be a good point for an ambush of the Llamhigyn Y Dwr water leapers. 
Once the PCs have the artefact, they can continue with it to St Cynogs in O08. To St Cynog’s (pg. 101), if 
following The Way of Blood, or travel back to Ynys Mons75 in O23. Dracones Venire (pg 142), if following 
The Way of Magic.  
 
 
75 Once the PCs have visited Ynys Mons for the first time, it is not considered necessary to have a bespoke Obstacle 
when they return. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
116 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O14. Finding Emrys 
Procedural Goal 
Allow the PCs to briefly be allied with Myrddin and give them the next step in their quest. 
Emotional Goal 
Give insight into the bigger picture – their fight here may affect all of Britain. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L6.1 Cyngreawdr Fynydd Mines, Rhos  
The mines on Cyngreawdr Fynydd have been abandoned since time out of mind, and a clan of Cythraul 
has lived for many years within the 10 miles of passages under the great headland. Emrys Nofather(C6.2) 
(Archdruid Myrddin) has been exploring the Mines. He had believed, mistakenly, that the copper leaching 
out of them may be related to the Blight.  
Emrys’ auguries have told him to expect a visitation by the ones who will raise The Blight, but that there 
will also be false representatives. Therefore, he needs to test those who come searching him out.  
The test Emrys sets is to recover an Artefact, the Brooch of Cúchulainn (A05), from the mines and bring 
it to him. He will warn the PCs that there is a tribe of Cythraul within the mines, and that any magic cast in 
their presence is likely to cause a Mishap or other unintentional effects. He will state that it is for this reason 
that he is sending the PCs instead of going himself. 
Artefacts: Emrys holds the Lleuad Cilgant (A09) moon sickle and in the bottom of the mine is the Brooch 
of Cúchulainn (A05) in location (4) 
Valuables: Hidden in the mine by a previous miner is a cache of 100 units of smelted Copper, (2), (worth 
1000 copper = 10 gold coins, and counts as 2 Heavy Items + 1 Light Item) 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
117 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
The Brooch has been taken by the Cythraul. The PCs will explore the mines and can experience hardships 
and tests of their skills – the climax occurs when the PCs hear guttural chanting coming from a low tunnel 
(3) in the far reaches of the mine. The tunnel opens in the floor, drops down, and then runs horizontally 
for a few dozen yards before opening upwards in the floor of the chamber beyond, (4). If the PCs crawl

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
118 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
through the tunnel76, it is wet with sea water, and they find a group of Cythraul in a space beyond that 
appears to be sacred to them. 
The fiends appear to be worshipping the Brooch on a makeshift altar, (4). The group includes several family 
units, including very young Cythraul children. The low chamber itself is wet and smells of the sea; it be-
comes immediately clear that the tide is rising rapidly through holes in the floor and that the low chamber 
will soon flood to the ceiling. As soon as the PCs arrive, then Cythraul will panic and try to make their way 
to the one exit, which already is starting to fill rapidly with water.    
As soon as the PCs try to get the Brooch, they find an old Cythraul blocking their path. Meanwhile a child 
Cythraul77 grabs the Brooch and crawls with it into a crevice at the far side of the chamber. Water now will 
be pouring into the entrance tunnel. It will fill the tunnel long before the chamber does, and the GM should 
start a countdown before the tunnel fills. Once the tunnel is filled, then those inside the chamber may 
drown.  
For the PCs it should be made clear when they must abandon efforts within the chamber to avoid drowning. 
There should be no skill checks necessary to avoid drowning if the PCs leave in good time. After that, the 
tunnel fills, and the rules per Drowning FLPG pg 113 should be used78.  
Choices 
The Child Cythraul positions the Brooch behind a small outcrop in the cramped crevice and then turns 
back to face the PCs, growling at them as it wedges itself in79. It is wedged so far into the crevice that 
although an adult may be able to pull it out, it will be badly hurt it in the process. Even then, getting to the 
Brooch ahead of the entrance tunnel flooding will require luck, magic, or both. Some choices 
• 
Pull the Child Cythraul out, hurting them in the process, and 
o carrying them out through the tunnel (pulling them behind or pushing them ahead). 
o leaving them to drown whilst concentrating on getting at the Brooch.  
• 
Use of Magic, for example to turn into a small animal to enter the crevice, entice or become a 
puppeteer to the Cythraul, or to wither or tear down the stone around it, will all work; however, 
the antimagic effect of the fiends make this option very dangerous. Depending on what method is 
used, the Cythraul Child might be harmed (see above) 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs pull out the Child but then leave it to concentrate exclusively on recovering the Brooch, 
then the Child will pull itself into another crevice and eventually be drowned. Emrys will be waiting 
at the mine entrance with the Cythraul tribe. As the PCs emerge, he will accept the Brooch but 
state that the PCs have failed the test. 
• 
If any of the PCs take the Child through the tunnel to safety, then they will find Emrys when they 
emerge from the mine. If not with the PCs, then he will enter and retrieve the Child and, if neces-
sary, tend to any wounds. Emrys will tell the PCs that they have passed the test, regardless of 
whether they recovered the Brooch. 
 
76 If they decide to lie in wait, then encourage them to enter by having a Cythraul emerge, be startled by the PCs, and 
then turn around and crawl back through the tunnel at top speed. 
77 Or if Safety Tools would prevent this, then a tiny adult Cythraul. 
78 It will require D6+3 successful Move tests, one per round, to pull themselves through the flooded tunnel. They will 
need to pass Endurance tests each round, and once they fail, then they will begin to take Strength Damage every 
round. Once Broken one cannot help themselves out. 
79 Threatening its parents, should they be found, will not persuade it to come out. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
119 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
If the PCs ask why Emry has done this, then he will say he needed to understand their character before 
deciding on whether to join forces with them. 
Rooting Interest 
None 
Next Steps 
If the PCs passed the test, then Emrys says that he will assist the PCs in this venture. If they have failed, he 
says that he cannot help them further, but he will still give them the information they require. 
Emrys will state that Abad Cadfan(C3.4) has captured an adult Cythraul and is torturing it in the dungeon 
under L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery.  
If following The Way of Blood, then Emrys will say that his auguries have told him that to both negate 
Maelgwn’s crimes and to end The Blight one must learn Abad Cadfan’s secrets from the captured Cythraul. 
This leads to O16. Free the Cythraul (pg 120). 
If following The Way of Magic, then Emrys will say that this Cythraul knows the locations of an Eagle-
stone that is key to removing The Blight from the land. This leads to O16. Free the Cythraul (pg 120). He will 
say that the Amulet must be brought to either Morgen(C2.4) on Ynys Enlli (L2.3) or Tobrecan(C4.4) on 
Ynys Mons (L4.6) – either of which who can tell the PCs what they must do to use the Amulet. He will, 
however, warn the PCs that each has their own agenda. 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
120 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O15. Tre’r Ceiri and Oron 
Procedural Goal 
Consulting with the ghost of Oron. 
Emotional Goal 
For the PCs to feel they have sacrificed something to gain their goal. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L1.4 Tre’r Ceiri, Afflogion 
Tre’r Ceiri (Settlement of Giants) sits on the eastern peak of Yr Eifl at 450m altitude. Inside its high walls 
there are the remains of over 150 roundhouses, and about 400 people once lived here. However, no one 
has dwelt here for at least 100 years, so now it is host to ravens, the odd sheep, a Gwrach y Rhibyn, and 
the Ghost of Oron(C1.4), former High Druid of Britain. Both Oron and the Gwrach y Rhibyn walk the 
ramparts at night. During the day Oron can be found in the small dungeon under the biggest of the col-
lapsed roundhouses. This dungeon is a small cell only 2m on a side, which must be squeezed into through 
a small hole in the floor above. 
 
Artefacts: Hidden within the abandoned roundhouses is an Artefact of Roman origin, a suit of Roman 
Lorica Lamminata Armour (A12). 
Valuables: Also with the suit of armour is a leather pouch with 10 gold coins. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
121 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
Anyone spending a night in the Hillfort will eventually come across both Oron(C1.4) and the Gwrach y 
Rhibyn. Once the PCs have sorted out who is who and removed the threat of the Gwrach y Rhibyn, then 
they can try and communicate with Oron, who is fixated in the guilt of being unable to save his fellow 
druids from the massacre over 400 years ago. Oron will respond to approaches from the PCs, but he feels 
no affinity for the living (he is not Manipulatable) and will tend to answer questions with nonsense verse.   
Choices 
Once the PCs have gleaned enough information to confirm who he is and what their request is, then some 
payments that Oron will suggest the following options, although he will consider suitable alternatives. 
1. Avenge him 
2. Share in his guilt 
3. Assuage his guilt 
Consequences 
1. Avenge Oron. Oron cares not that Segontium is long gone, now replaced by L3.2 Caer Segeint. 
Oron requests that the PCs bring down the walls of the fort. Although this sounds like a difficult 
task, Oron does not care if a small part of the wall is brought down vs all the walls. However, doing 
so, without attracting the wrath of the Rhi Cadwallon(C3.5) will be the bigger issue.    
Tre’r Ceiri Hillfort

(2) Postern Gates (access to springs)

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
122 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
2. Share in Oron’s Guilt. One PC must agree to accept a portion of Oron’s guilt and knowledge. 
The process will irrevocably age the PC, although they will gain knowledge at the same time. Once 
the process is complete the PC increases one age bracket (Thus it would kill an Old human) with 
attendant impacts on Attributes, Skills, and Talents, but not Reputation. They also take the White 
Hair and Nightmares Critical Injuries (FLPG pg 199), as the horror of event now sits with them. 
3. Assuage Oron’s Guilt. The PCs must agree to lay Oron’s comrades properly to rest. They must 
travel to the Druid’s grove on Ynys Mons, L4.3 Llwyn o Gerrig, where the massacred Druids’ 
cremated remains lie – these remains must be taken via Senex Vaie (pg. 170) to Annwn. Oron feels 
that only this way will his comrades be able to rest. Note: Oron will state that the odds are high 
that whoever takes the remains will not return80. 
Rooting Interest 
Sharing Oron’s Guilt is the quickest option, and possibly the least dangerous, although it requires a sacrifice 
by a PC. 
Next Steps 
Once Oron’s price has been paid then he will grant the petitioners the knowledge they seek.  
• 
If following The Way of Blood, Oron will tell the PCs that Cadfan’s terrible secret is that he has 
created a Grim to protect his monastery (He relates the same details as listed in O16. Free the 
Cythraul Next Steps (pg 124)). The pain experienced by this Grim can be felt by all those who exist 
partly in Annwn, such as himself. This leads to O21. The Grim (pg 136). 
• 
If following The Way of Magic, Oron will tell the PCs that the Eaglestone must be thrust into 
the Well of the World, which is situated withing L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu on Ynys Mons. This leads 
to O23. Dracones Venire (pg. 142) 
• 
If following The Way of Spirit, Oron will tell the PCs that Caer Sidi has to be called into being 
from Annwn. One place this can be done is L3.7 Llyn Gwynant, a small lake to the northeast of 
L3.6 Dinas Affaraon near the tref of L3.5 Cwm Dyli, in Arfon Cantref. They should seek out a 
shepherd81 who tends his flocks there and who lives in a hut on a bluff overlooking the lake. This 
leads to O17. Caer Sidi and Custennin (pg 125). 
 
 
80 Noting that the Greal Sanctaidd may help in this endeavour. A Canny PCs may also find a way to get a Druid NPC 
to take the risk for them. 
81 Oron does not mention that this shepherd is a giant! 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
123 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O16. Free the Cythraul 
Procedural Goal 
Reveal Cadfan’s weakness (The Way of Blood) or reveal the location of an Eaglestone (The Way of Magic). 
Emotional Goal 
Allow the PCs to consider beings of Magic as more than just monsters. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery, Arfon 
Below Saint Cynog’s the Abad Cadfan(C3.4) has been holding an adult male Cythraul, named Llawgair(C3.8) 
(Oath), in the old dungeon from the time when it was a hillfort. The dungeon is a simple affair involving a 
short underground passage and then a hole in the floor which drops 3m into a lower chamber.  
Cadfan captured the Cythraul during one of Emrys’(C6.2) visits to Maelgwn, the creature having followed 
Emrys from the mines on L6.1 Cyngreawdr Fynydd. Cadfan believes it is a devil as described in the Book 
of Revelations, and now he occasionally tortures it, trying to work out its links to the Great Red Dragon, 
with seven heads, then horns, seven, crowns, and a huge tail.   
The PCs will know of Llawgair’s presence via Emrys and be trying to free it, likely with Emrys’ assistance. 
This could be played out as a Heist, with the PCs trying to sneak in and sneak the Cythraul out, an Assault, 
where they try and force their way in, or possibly using bargaining or Manipulation methods to free the 
beast.  
Note: Prince Maelgwn(C3.10) still will likely be present and possibly the child Buan (from O08). 
Note: Those acting physically against the monks will bring the Grim onto themselves (see O21. The Grim 
(pg 136)). If the PCs assaults any Monks, then the Grim appears in 1D6 rounds and attacks them. 
Artefact: Cadfan holds Saint Cynog’s Gilded Armilla (A14) 
Dilemma 
Once freed from its chains, Llawgair will take the first opportunity to attack and kill one of the monks. If 
it succeeds in disabling the person, it will immediately stop to try to feed. This will also bring out the Grim. 
Choices 
The PCs can try and stop the beast from attacking the monk, or they can let it feed. 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs try and stop Llawgair, then they may have attack it or at least treat it roughly. In doing 
so, they will make it materially more difficult to get the information they need from the beast 
(Adjust subsequent Manipulation rolls accordingly).  
• 
Helping a Cythraul escape is one thing, but helping a Cythraul kill and feed on Christians is quite 
another. The reputation of the PCs should increase by 1 and social interactions in Arfon and Rhos 
after this event may be badly affected (-2D or more). 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
124 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Rooting Interest 
The PCs desire to get information from the Cythraul, vs the consequences of letting a beast feed on humans. 
Next Steps 
Once Llawgair is freed, then the PCs can attempt to extract the information they need. They should always 
be able to get the information, however the circumstances will affect the how easy it is to do so.  
• 
If Llawgair has been returned to the mines of L6.1 Cyngreawdr Fynydd, with its brethren and if its 
hunger for Christian flesh has been slaked, then it will simply tell them what they want to know.  
• 
If Llawgair has been badly treated, tortured by the PCs, or is otherwise in a position to distrust 
them, then the PCs will need to pass Manipulation vs Insight tests and if failed will have to carry 
out other tasks to gain the trust of the beast. These tasks could include scaring off local Britons 
who have been attacking the Cythraul band, finding a new safe home for the band, or securing 
food for the band.  
Once Llawgair has been placated82, then it will tell the PCs what they want to know:  
If following The Way of Blood, then Llawgair will tell the PCs Abad Cadfan’s secret. In the long hours 
when the Abad was torturing the beast, he would talk and confess his sins, whilst he applied the hot irons 
and pulled out its claws. One thing he told it was that in order for Saint Cynog’s to thrive he was forced to 
subdue the old gods – he consulted his copy of the Mystic Gospel of Mark and learned that by burying 
alive a pagan woman under the hillfort, that her ghost would protect it forever more as a Grim83. He duly 
did so, drugging one of his servants, a Briton named Tymyr, before gagging and binding her and then 
sealing her unconscious form in a hole under the northeastern battlements, with the help of Tydecho(C3.14) 
and Cynllo(C3.7). Although they piled many rocks over the entrance, a few moans could be distantly heard 
in the following days, and now that corner of the walls is said to be haunted by the servants.   
The GM should tell the players that on hearing this story that several things are immediately obvious, given 
their understanding of The Blight gained during their adventures. That being...  
If the ghost of this woman was put to rest84, then: 
• 
this might raise The Blight,  
• 
the mythic protection enjoyed by the Christians would be removed. 
If, additionally, Cadfan’s crime against the pagans was proven then this would: 
• 
set the Rhiau, including, Einon, against Cadfan. 
• 
set his own people against Cadfan and destroy his powerbase amongst the Christians. 
Llawgair can tell the PCs exactly where Tymyr’s body lies within L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery, and so 
the PCs should have a solid lead to O21. The Grim (pg 136). 
If following The Way of Magic, then Llawgair, will reveal to the PCs where an Eaglestone may be found. 
This is in a cave on the cliffs just below L3.4 Carnedd y Cawr and leads to O18. Anger the Dragon (pg 129). 
 
82 If the Cythraul is killed, then on of the Monks or Servants can be the source of the information needed. 
83 Like the English Folklore of a Church Grim. 
84 A proper pagan funeral would involve burning her remains on a pyre and then interring her ashes. See Funerals (pg 
120). 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
125 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O17. Caer Sidi and Custennin 
Procedural Goal 
Allow the PCs to recover Cernunnos Torc 
Emotional Goal 
Escalation for the Way of Spirit. Show the PCs the power and mysteriousness of Annwn and hint at the 
dangers of the Senex Vaie. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.7 Llyn Gwynant, Arfon 
The small lake of Llyn Gwynant is nestled amongst the Eryri mountains, features a rocky bluff which rises 
out of the water on the northwest side. At the top of this bluff lives Custennin(C3.6) the giant in a crude and 
quite large hut. He shepherds a large flock of sheep and keeps to himself. The locals in L3.5 Cwm Dyli 
leave him alone, and he them. The PCs will be searching for a shepherd, but they will likely be surprised to 
find out it is a giant. 
Custennin is generally benevolent and helpful, however if attacked he will defend himself robustly. The PCs 
will need to convince Custennin to allow them entry to Caer Sidi (or disable him and take his key), but the 
true dilemma will become apparent when they try to take Cernunnos’ Torc. Custennin’s motives are for 
maintaining a balance with Annwn by following Fae Law85, and the protection of Gwynedd generally and 
his flock and this valley in particular. Ways to win over the giant include:  
• 
A contest of wits (Riddling Game, opposed Insight) 
• 
A contest of strength (A Feat, opposed Might) 
• 
A contest of Poetry or Song (Eisteddfod, opposed Performance)  
If the PCs fail, then keep trying different contests until they succeed. 
Custennin, if treated well by the PCs, will warn them that the gold torc inside is protected. The torc is 
contained in a bronze box, something that the immortals of Annwn, especially Cernunnos, cannot touch 
without great pain. More, any immortal opening the box will give up a portion of their immortality. Any 
mortal on the other hand will be given a portion of immortality. He will say this aspect is not a gift that it 
might first appear.  
Accessing Caer Sidi 
"The strong-doored, foursquare fortress of glass, manned by its dumb, ghostly sentinels, spun round in never-ceasing revolution, 
so that few could find its entrance; it was pitch-dark save for the twilight made by the lamp burning before its circling gate; 
feasting went on there, and revelry, and in its centre, choicest of its many riches, was the pearl-rimmed cauldron of poetry and 
inspiration, kept bubbling by the breaths of nine British pythonesses, so that it might give forth its oracles." Charles Squire, 
The Mythology of the British Islands 
On the nearby clifftop sits a large oak tree, which leans out over the dark waters of the llyn. The tree is 
the lock which prevents Caer Sidi from rising from the depths of the lake. This legendary fortress exists in 
Annwn, and Custennin has been charged with guarding this point of access. If the iron key around the 
 
85 A vague and inconsistent list of typical fae rules such as: never lie, be polite, trade rather than take, respect 
where it is due, and similar. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
126 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Giant’s neck is carried to the tree and dropped into a hole in its bole, then the waters of the llyn will start 
to boil and thrash, a vortex will form, and out of the water will rise a square fortress of black obsidian glass, 
revolving destra (to the right). The rising Caer Sidi will stop when its front gate is level with the top of the 
cliff and a wooden bridge will drop down and close the gap. 
Over the gate burns a bright oil lamp. Those striding over the gap find the surroundings become darker, 
the sky a purple bruise. At each corner of Caer Sidi stands a mute and ghostly sentinel, dressed for war. 
Inside a feast is in full swing, as fae immortals engage in every kind of loud merriment and revelry – how-
ever, as solid as they seem, they are only half-way between Annwn and Mortal lands. They do not see the 
PCs and their bodies appear insubstantial. In the centre of the fortress rises a black glass tower with no 
entrance, there are only banister-less steps rising in a helix around the outside. Anyone climbing the vertig-
inous stairs finds at the top a flat space with a large pearl-rimmed cauldron and a bronze pillar fixed in the 
glass floor.  
The pearl-rimmed cauldron bubbles with a frothy insubstantial liquid. This liquid is Pure Inspiration in 
physical form. Anyone who drinks of it will find they gain 1 point in Performance when they leave Caer 
Sidi. 
The small pillar is covered with bewildering and beautiful shapes which befuddle those who look at them 
too long, and the top flutes out to form a box with a small door. Inside the bronze box lies Cernunnos’ 
Torc (A02).  
 
Caer Sidi appears 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
127 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
The Torc is protected by the magic of Annwn, but the protection is primarily aimed at foiling the residents 
of that place, not of mortals. Mortals can easily open the box, but anyone removing Cernunnos’ Torc will 
have some of their humanity removed. 
Choices 
One of the PCs must volunteer to take the torc. 
(1) Drawbridge to cliff-edge 
(2) Courtyard with ghostly fae 
(3) Glass Pillar 
(4) Entrance towers 
(5) Sentinel towers 
Note: Directions and distances are meaningless 
Caer Sidi 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
128 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Consequences 
The person who removes the torc from the box will gain the Knowledge of the locations of, and the ability 
to Open and safely Walk, the Senex Vaie (pg 170) without straying, but there is a downside. They will take 
a permanent 1-point loss to Empathy. Additionally, if they are Adult or Old, they will drop an Age Bracket 
(with attendant effects on Attributes, Skills, and Talents). Alternatively, if they are Young, they become 
Sullen (FLPG pg 199) for 3D6 days. 
Rooting Interest 
None 
Next Steps 
Once the torc is removed from the box the PCs may leave Caer Sidi, as they do so the fortress begins 
spinning sinistra (to the left) and sinks under the water. 
If Custennin is alive he will be waiting for the PCs to return. He will ask them what they will do with the 
torc. If they tell him they are taking it to Morgen, then he will warn them that she wants it for her own 
purposes. He will suggest that Tobrecan(C4.4) of Sancta Arcis (move to O07. To Ynys Mons (pg 99)) or 
Maelgwn(C3.10) of Saint Cynog’s (move to O08. To St Cynog’s (pg 101)) will know what must be done with 
the torc. 
If the PCs tell him something else, or if Custennin is unable to ask the question for any reason, then they 
likely will return to Morgen to offer her the torc, but they will be less well equipped to deal with her. You 
can use aspects of O22. Interception (pg. 138), but have her reveal the information that the torc must be 
placed in the well in L4.2 Bryn Celli Ddu and then develop obstacles to suit the situation. 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
129 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O18. Anger the Dragon 
Procedural Goal 
Allow the PCs to recover the Eaglestone Amulet 
Emotional Goal 
Provide the Escalation for The Way of Magic 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.4 Carnedd y Cawr, Eryi Mountains, Arfon 
Upon the high mountain of Carnedd y Cawr there is a large cairn, used by the druids for their judgements, 
but also nearby there is a Dragons’ Lair.  
The lair opens out on a sheer cliff face below the cairn; entry requires someone to climb86 (or be lowered 
down) to the opening. The opening itself is wide and high with a dry sandy floor. The cave leads back into 
the darkness, and this is where the two dragons, Gwenllian and her mate Aurddu, shelter. Inside the cave 
is also their treasure; there is gold, but that which they value most is an Eaglestone bound into an amulet. 
The dragons sleep deeper in the cave in a chamber with an entrance just wide enough for them to slither 
in through. They have no concept that someone might invade their space, and they prefer to sleep the days 
away in the darker depths of the cave. 
Closer to the cliff face there is a nursery chamber with one of their dragonling young and another with 
the Eaglestone Amulet in a battered wooden bowl, along with gold coins. They have left some food for 
their young, in the form of a badly injured villager, Ina.  
Artefacts: The dragons have Eaglestone Amulet (A06) in their cave lair. 
Valuables: 25 gold coins are in the bowl with the Eaglestone amulet. 
 
 
 
 
86 A relatively easy feat (Simple (+2) Move Test), but the associated vertigo also causes a 2D Fear Attack, and a failure 
of the Move test, if not protected by a rope, leads to death. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
130 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
Sneaking into a Dragon’s Lair is one thing, but taking its treasure and returning is quite another. To com-
plicate matters, inside the lair the PCs come across a wounded villager, Ina – she has been brought here 
and left by the dragons as food for their young. She is badly wounded (Broken, with Punctured Lung, lethal 
in 1D6 days) and has been left near the entrance chamber (2) in the knowledge that she cannot climb out 
and is no threat to the dragonling. 
(1) Cliffside entrance 
(2) Dragonling Nursery 
(3) Ina the wounded villager 
(4) Eaglestone Amulet 
(5) Gwenllian & Aurddu’s sleeping chamber 
Dragons’ Lair 
1 
4
