0m 
5m 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
131 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
The dragonling has recently been fed and generally keeps to its chamber (3), unless the PCs make a lot of 
noise or tarry for a long time. Stats: small female dragonling (Strength 6, Agility 4, Claw Attack with 5D 
and 1 damage)87.  
Choices 
The PCs can rescue the villager, or they can continue with their mission to find and retrieve the Eaglestone. 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs decide to rescue the villager, Ina, then there is a significant risk of alerting Gwenllian to 
the PCs’ presence in moving the badly injured Ina out of the cave and up the cliff-face (Average 
Difficulty Stealth (0D) becomes Formidable (-3D)).  
o If alerted, the dragon’s primary goal will be to drive out the PCs from its lair, rather than 
kill them.  
o Also, the PCs will notice that the Eaglestone Amulet begins to vibrate and rattle in the 
Dragon’s presence and that the Dragon shies away and avoids anyone carrying the Eagle-
stone Amulet88.  
o Regardless, this should be a tense encounter that may test the PC’s abilities. In the unlikely 
event that the PCs have the upper hand, then Gwenllian’s mate Aurrdu may arrive back 
from hunting to push the odds.   
• 
If the PCs leave the villager, Ina, to her fate, then they will hear her agonising screams as she is 
eaten alive by the dragonling. 
Rooting Interest 
Self-preservation and the importance of the quest vs basic humanity 
Next Steps 
Presuming the PCs manage to steal the Eaglestone Amulet and climb back out up the clifftop, possibly 
with a badly injured villager in tow, then they should hear terrible roars and see gouts of flame from the top 
of the mountain as they descend.  
From this point onwards random encounters will trigger a Dragon sighting on a 11 through 16 on the 
Random Encounters table (pg 166), and on a 66 the Dragon finds the PCs. In this case it would initially harass 
the PCs before leaving to find its mate. Again, due to their fear of anyone in possession of an Eaglestone, 
the Dragons will appear strangely reluctant to engage with the PCs. There should be time for the PCs to 
evade and escape the two Dragons before their return.  
From O14. Finding Emrys (pg 119) the PCs will know that either Morgen or Tobrecan can tell them what 
they need to do with the Eaglestone Amulet. This will lead to O09. The Ferryman (pg 102) if the PCs decide 
to go to Morgen89, or to O07. To Ynys Mons (pg 99) if the PCs try Tobrecan90. 
 
87 If the Dragonling is killed by the actions of the PCs, then any subsequent interactions should take this into account. 
The Dragons would react with extreme prejudice to the PCs after such an action. 
88 Due to their fear of the stones’ effect on them. 
89 Or directly to O12. An Audience at Caer Wydyr (pg 86) if the PCs have begun to use the risky Senex Vaie. 
90 Again, or directly to O10. An Audience at Sancta Arcis (pg 82) if the PCs use the Senex Vaie. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
132 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O19. Maelgwn’s Downfall 
Procedural Goal 
Put the PCs on the path to finding and freeing the Grim from St Cynog’s, either via Emrys or Morgen. 
Emotional Goal 
Provide the Escalation for The Way of Blood. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery, Arfon 
The PCs will have searched out and found the Lancea Longini (A08) and believe that Maelgwn(C3.10) will 
use it to rid the land of The Blight91. After travelling to Saint Cynog’s, and gaining access to Maelgwn, 
they may be feeling victory is within their grasp.   
However, the curse of the Lancea Longini, means that as soon as Maelgwn grasps the artefact he will be 
compelled to kill those around him of the Christian faith. Even if he is not in the confines of Saint Cynog’s 
when he is given the artefact, then the PCs will be expecting to bring him into their presence. Therefore, at 
some point Maelgwn will attempt murder and will do everything in his power to do so on as large a scale 
as possible. 
Note: The bearer of the Lancea Longini is largely safe from the Grim (see O21. The Grim (pg 136)), but if 
the PCs themselves act against the Monks, then the Grim appears in 1D6 rounds and attacks them. 
Dilemma 
It will soon become clear that a teenage princeling killing defenceless monks is not going to remove The 
Blight from the land. If he is in Saint Cynog’s, the guards will soon encircle him and attempt to disable him. 
If the PCs join in, it could become a massacre. 
Choices 
• 
Join in with Maelgwn on his killing spree 
• 
Join the guards in trying to calm down Maelgwn and stop the bloodshed 
• 
Run away 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs join in with Maelgwn, then they may be able to take out quite a few guards, but now 
monks will begin to join in, and the PCs should feel that they are about to be overwhelmed. At this 
point Abad Cadfan(C3.4) or Tydecho(C3.14) should intervene and scream for a halt. Maelgwn, dis-
tracted, is then grappled by several guards or monks. The PCs will be considered murderers along 
with the princeling. 
• 
If the PCs join with the guards, then Maelgwn should be grappled and held after a short struggle. 
The PCs may be considered complicit or considered heroes, depending on the circumstances. 
 
91 If Maelgwn(C3.10) is dead or incapacitated, then the PCs can find another of Royal Blood, say a sister. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
133 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
• 
If the PCs run away, then Maelgwn will eventually be overcome and subdued by the guards and 
monks. The PCs will be considered complicit, and a call go out to make them Outlaw. 
Rooting Interest 
The PCs will likely have no love for the Monks and want to see their downfall. On the other hand, they 
likely will see no benefit in just trying to murder all the Christians. It should also be clear that Maelgwn’s 
actions will be unlikely to help the tense political situation in Gwynedd at this time. 
Next Steps 
Note: This is a significant setback for the PCs -the way forward from this mess should be presented to the 
PCs early and with conviction – it should be a down-beat with an up-beat as soon as possible afterwards. 
See below. 
That a princeling of the land has martyred monks of Saint Cynogs is a significant boost for the Christian 
faction in Gwynedd. They can demand Weregild from Cadwallon(C3.5) (see T7 on the Timeline & Faction 
Map (pg 80)), and Einon and Cadfan’s moral position will also be strengthened, whilst their relationships 
with Arfon deteriorates.  
The Blight will respond in turn, with wells drying up, plants shrivelling, and other visible signs of distress 
in the land. Maelgwn, if still alive, will return to his old self once the Lancea Longini is taken from him92, 
and he will be stricken by grief at his actions and his murder of his friends. 
• 
If the PCs are imprisoned at Saint Cynog’s, then Maelgwn, if still alive, or the young monk Pa-
darn(C3.12) a friend of Maelgwn’s, will tell the PCs that his friend Emrys(C6.2), who lives in the old 
copper mines of L6.1 Cyngreawdr Fynydd, is one who can right the wrong that was done here. 
This leads to O14. Finding Emrys (pg 116). 
• 
If the PCs are free, then the young monk Padarn(C3.12) a friend of Maelgwn’s, seeks out the PCs. 
He tells them that he has had a puzzling dream, where a Stag appeared to him. He understood the 
Stag, which told him to find the PCs and plead with them to find Morgen(C2.4) on Ynys Enlli (L2.3), 
for without her guidance Maelgwn’s actions will come to ill for all. This leads to O09. The Ferryman 
(pg 102). 
O20. Illtud The Hermit 
Procedural Goal 
Provide the PCs with the information on what to do with the Eaglestone Amulet and how it can be used 
against Dragons. 
Emotional Goal 
Give the opportunity for the PCs to interact and cooperate with Christians in a non-confrontational way. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L4.7 Ynys Lannog, of Ynys Mons 
 
92 The monks will likely take the Lancea Longini and sequester it in St Cynogs’ Vault. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
134 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
The small island of Ynys Lannog sits off the northeast tip of Ynys Mons. On its bare flanks a few scraggly 
sheep munch grasses and there sits a small, stone beehive hut. Illtud the Hermit(C4.2) lives there. His only 
possession and his concession to the clinging to worldly things is the Codex Matris (A04), which he keeps 
in a small dry cave at the northeast tip of the island. 
The currents are swift and getting to the island will require a coracle or other small boat – possibly from 
the nearby Tref of L4.4 Pant-Y-Saer. 
When the PCs arrive on the island they will find, Illtud the hermit standing on a rock, and intoning Chris-
tian Scripture in Latin. For example: “Tu scis necessitatem meam, quod abominer signum superbiae et gloriae meae, 
quod est super caput meum in diebus ostentationis meae, et detester illud quasi pannum menstruatae, et non portem in diebus 
silentii mei,”93. He will keep his distance and will initially try to keep any interactions with the PCs to a 
minimum, but he will admit to being a Christian Hermit. 
Artefacts: Codex Matrix (A04) hidden in a cave. 
Dilemma 
Illtud is ill disposed toward visitors, especially any visitors who appear to be pagans. The PCs will need to 
find a way to convince him to let them see his prized, hidden, and only possession. 
Choices 
• 
Force – the PCs could threaten Illtud to convince him to show them the Codex 
• 
Guile – the PCs could attempt to trick Illtud into showing them the Codex, 
• 
Appeasement – the PCs could attempt to bargain with Illtud to convince him to show them the 
Codex 
Consequences 
• 
Depending upon whether Illtud leaves the encounter feeling tricked or forced, will determine how 
much help he provides in deciphering the Codex’s statements on the Eaglestones, and how this 
related to “the King”. See Next Steps below. 
Rooting Interest 
The PCs will likely have no love of the Christians by this point; however, they also will have reasons to 
distrust Tobrecan, meaning they will know they need to tread carefully. 
Next Steps 
Once the PCs have the information in the Codex (see Codex Matris (A04)), then this is not sufficient on 
its own to answer their question of how to use the Eaglestone Amulet to halt The Blight.  
• 
If Illtud is inclined to help the PCs, then he will offer advice as to the meaning of the paragraph 
on Eaglestones in the Codex. He will tell the PCs that the “well” associated with the Eaglestones 
is the Well of the World, which sits at the centre of L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu, an ancient mound on 
 
93 “You know my need, because I hate the sign of pride and of my glory, which is upon my head in the days of display mine, and you will 
hate it as a menstrual cloth, and not a porter in it in the days of my silence” 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
135 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Ynys Mons. He will state that the “King” discussed in the text does not refer to Cadwallon or the 
other Rhiau, but rather to Tobrecan who styles himself as leader of Ynys Mons. 
• 
If Illtud feels neutrally towards the PCs, then he will tell the PCs that the well refers to a place 
in L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu, but provide no other information. 
• 
If Illtud feels antagonistic towards the PCs, then he will proffer no information. 
Regardless of the information provided by Illtud, the PCs now should have enough information to lead 
them to Bryn Ceilli Ddu and O23. Dracones Venire (pg 142). They may, however, need to seek out more 
information from other sources of Lore94, if they are going to identify the location of the well if they have 
antagonised Illtud. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
94 Tobrecan or the Druids are the most likely suspects. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
136 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O21. The Grim 
Procedural Goal 
Lay the Grim to rest, break the power of Abad Cadfan, and hopefully halt The Blight. 
Emotional Goal 
Climax to the Campaign 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery, Arfon 
The PCs now know Abad Cadfan’s secret – shortly before the onset of The Blight Cadfan(C3.4) created a 
Grim to protect Saint Cynog’s by sacrificing a pagan woman called Tymyr, burying her alive under the old 
hillfort’s northeast foundations. He keeps this act secret from everyone.  
If the PCs give the woman a proper pagan burial, then this might do more than just break the Grim’s 
protection of St Cynog’s, it might break The Blight. Further, if they can prove that it was Cadfan who did 
this deed, then his political power in Gwynedd will be dealt a heavy blow. 
The PCs will likely need to surmount several obstacles before they are able to exhume the body of Tymyr 
– be it a heist, force of arms, or use of guile – eventually they will be in a position to unearth her remains. 
Dilemma 
Tymry is now a Grim, and her power acts against all those who consider themselves enemies of Cadfan 
and the Christians. Those trying to unearth her body will find the Grim stealing up behind them and come 
under attack. At the same time the PCs may be suffering from interventions from the monk and guards, 
and Cadfan(C3.4) or Tydecho(C3.14) may appear wielding the Lancea Longini (A08). 
Choices 
Choices include, combat, persuasion, and bargaining, however the PCs will likely have to destroy the Grim, 
temporarily, if they are to be able to exhume the body. 
Consequences 
As the Grim expires, several monks including Cynllo(C3.7), if still alive, will arrive. Cynllo will break down 
and confess his part in the sacrifice, begging the forgiveness of his god and of his colleagues.  
Rooting Interest 
None. 
Next Steps 
If the Grim’s remains are cremated and interred as befitting a pagan (see Funerals (pg 153)) then the burial 
site fills with a golden light – the PCs feel themselves becoming lighter as their burdens ease (Equivalent to 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
137 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
a Quarter Day’s Resting, with all Broken conditions removed and one point restored to the relevant Attrib-
ute). The sun will come out and at the same time a cooling rain will begin to fall. Move to The Resolution (pg 
143). 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
138 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O22. Interception 
Procedural Goal 
Allow the PCs to deposit Cernunnos’ Torc in Bryn Ceilli Ddu and hopefully halt The Blight. 
Emotional Goal 
Climax of the Campaign 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu, Ynys Mons 
The PCs are travelling to Bryn Celli Ddu with 
Cernunnos’ Torc. They are intent on destroy-
ing it there, if following The Way of Magic, 
or placing it in the well in the centre, if follow-
ing The Way of Spirit. However, Mor-
gen(C2.4) has other ideas. She needs the torc as 
a bargaining chip to offer to the goddess An-
draste in return for her retrieving her beloved 
brother Mordred from Annwn.  
Note: See the Timeline & Faction Map (pg. 80), if Cadwallon has sent an invasion force to Ynys Mons by this 
point (possible with T9), then Ambaxtos Warriors of both sides may be encountered on a war footing on 
Ynys Mons. 
 
The Well of the World. This is a pit situated in the 
centre of the mound of Bryn Celli Ddu. Over the pit, 
floating in mid-air, is the Pattern Stone, a cap that sep-
arates the power of Annwn from that of Magic and 
the World. The pit is 1m in diameter, and within its 
dark interior a pearlescent, creamy liquid can be dimly 
made out. Objects placed in the Well, including hu-
mans, will be transported to Annwn. If Cernunnos’ 
Torc or the Eaglestone Amulet is placed in the Well, 
then additionally the Well will be lit by a golden light 
and the Pattern Stone will descend to cap it. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
139 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
 
Dilemma 
As the PCs approach Bryn Ceilli Ddu then Morgen makes herself known. The ambient light will appear to 
dim, either the sun or torches, and sounds of the surroundings will fade. Morgen will step into the silence 
that remains and show herself. She will initially appear to be friendly and agreeable, but upon any hesitancy 
from the PCs she will soon lose out to impatience. She will demand that the PCs hand over Cernunnos’ 
Torc. In classic pulp-villain fashion, she will state that “…rather than destroy the torc in Bryn Ceilli Ddu, you fools 
simply needed to place it in the well in the centre. That would return it to Cernunnos and restore his power. Instead, I will offer 
it to Andraste and she will return to the living my brother Mordred!”. 
(1) Ditch 
(2) Standing Stones 
(3) Kerbed Mound 
(4) Entrance & Ghost Fence 
(5) Pillar, Pattern Stone, and Well of the World 
N 
0m 
10m 
Bryn Celli Ddu 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
140 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
At this point the PCs should notice that silent rows of large hares and white stags95 have assembled, waiting 
and watching the proceedings. 
The PCs are likely now so competent that Morgen and her sisters would not feel themselves able to handle 
them on their own. She will have used Primal Soul96 to enlist the help of Lluan Knackercrusher(C3.9) and 
her warriors, either taken from the invasion force (T8) or brought over at Morgen’s request. 
The PCs should also know by now that Morgen wants the torc for her own ends (from Emry’s warning in 
O11. An Audience at St Cynog’s, Next Steps (pg 109) or Tobrecan’s warning in O10. An Audience at Sancta 
Arcis, Next Steps (pg 106))). If they give it up, they may not see it again. 
Choices 
The PCs can Refuse Morgen’s request, try to Bargain with her, or Acquiesce. However, unknown to the 
PCs the forces of Cernunnos are also at play now that his Torc is so close to this place of power. 
Consequences 
• 
If the PCs refuse, Morgen will attack. See below.  
• 
If the PCs bargain, the only solution that Morgen will accept is her brother brought back to life. If 
the PCs can come up with a plausible strategy to do so97 and IF it appears to be more likely to 
succeed than her current strategy98, then she may consider their offer. In the case that they come 
to an agreement, then she will not let the torc out of her sight and she will ensure that her sisters 
are always with her whilst the PCs plans unfold. 
• 
If the PCs acquiesce, then Morgen will shriek with delight and victory, take the torc, and immedi-
ately begin to make her way away from Bryn Ceilli Ddu and into the forest where she plans to carry 
out the ritual to offer the torc to Andraste. The Deri-Ednyf will attack. See below. 
The Attack 
If Morgen attacks:  
□ She calls out, and two ravens will flap in from nearby trees. These will shape-shift into her two 
sisters, Morgause(C2.3) and Blasine(C2.2).  
□ At the same time Lluan Knackercrusher(C3.9) and some of her Ambaxtos Warriors99 will also 
march out of the mound entrance and from beneath the nearby trees to support Morgen. Lluan 
will apologise to the PCs as she and her warriors rush to attack.  
□ The two sisters will furiously assault the PCs using Bear’s Claw and Beastmaster spells (bringing 
Wild Boars in to attack the PCs).  
□ Meanwhile Morgen will use Dispel Magic to minimise the magical powers of the PCs and Primal 
Soul to terrify and rout any PC at low Wits. Play the sisters as deviously as possible – they are 
intelligent foes who will take every advantage they can to win. 
 
95 Sacred animals to Andraste and Cernunnos respectively. 
96 Morgen does this by using the power of the spell to convince Lluan that the PCs are working against the Christians 
of Gwynedd and that Morgen must take the Torc away. 
97 Perhaps involving ill-advised forays into Annwn to find Mordred and return with him. 
98 Say because the PCs offer a trump card that she has not considered. However, the PCs suggestion must appear to 
her to be more likely of success than simply taking the torc from the PCs (she justifiably feels the odds are in her 
favour). If she has any doubts about the PCs ability to achieve this then she will reject the bargain. 
99 Exact numbers at the discretion of the GM. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
141 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
□ If Morgen’s forces appear to be getting the upper hand, then a tearing, 
soughing sound will be heard and the trees themselves around the clearing 
will begin to move, ripping their roots from the soil as they turn on Mor-
gen’s forces. These are Oak tree-spirits, Deri-Ednyf, defending Cernun-
nos99. 
□ This more evenly matched fight will now progress until the PCs prevail or 
Morgen does. 
If Morgen accepts the torc and begins to make her way into the forest: 
□ Suddenly a tearing, soughing sound will be heard and the trees themselves 
around the clearing will begin to move, ripping their roots from the soil as 
they lumber towards Morgen99. 
□ At the same time Morgen’s sisters and Lluan and her warriors appear, and 
a standoff ensues. The warriors only avoid being routed by the force of 
Lluan’s presence. Morgen will attempt to escape.  
□ The PCs at this point will be ignored by Morgen’s Sisters and Lluan’s War-
riors as they concentrate on the lumbering Deri-Ednyf. The PCs can try to 
intercept Morgen and prevent her from carrying out a short ritual to dedi-
cate the Torc to Andraste.   
Rooting Interest 
The PCs are incentivised to refuse Morgen, but also know that her power makes her a formidable foe. 
Next Steps 
If Morgen is defeated, then the PCs can take Cernunnos’ Torc and place it in the Well of the World in the 
centre of L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu to complete their quest. The well fills will a golden light and then the Pattern 
Stone above it slowly descends to cover the aperture. The PCs feel themselves becoming lighter as their 
burdens ease (Equivalent to a Quarter Day’s Resting, with all Broken conditions removed and one point 
restored to the relevant Attribute). Venturing outside, the PCs will find the Morgen’s forces gone, the trees 
back in the ground, and a cooling rain is falling. Move to The Resolution (pg 143). 
If Morgen is allowed to escape, then she will carry out the ritual using her scrying bowl (A10). Andraste will 
accept the Torc, Mordred will return, and he and Morgen will return to Ynys Enlli. At this point the PCs 
best option would be to return to Bodhmall(C3.3) to see if another Way is open to them.  
 
 
 
 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
142 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
O23. Dracones Venire 
Procedural Goal 
Allow the PCs to deposit the Eaglestone Amulet in Bryn Ceilli Ddu and hopefully halt The Blight. 
Emotional Goal 
Climax to the Campaign. 
Setting the Scene 
Location: L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu, Ynys MonsFor more details on the 
site, see O22. Interception (pg 138)). 
The PCs are now on Ynys Mons and travelling to the site of Bryn 
Ceilli Ddu with the purpose of depositing the Eaglestone Amulet 
(A06) there. However, the dragons Gwenllian and Aurrdu suspect the 
PC’s will bring their treasure here and are lying in wait. As the PCs 
approach the sacred site of Bryn Ceilli Ddu, or as they appear from 
the Senex Vaie, their Eaglestone Amulet begins to vibrate and audibly 
rattle100.  
With an echoing roar the dragon Gwenllian leaps clear over the 
mound of Bryn Ceilli Ddu, landing in front of the one entrance. 
Dilemma 
Gwenllian blocks the PC’s way, whilst Aurrdu remains in hiding, attempting to circle quietly behind the 
PCs (Unless the PCs are actively looking around for danger use an Opposed Stealth vs Scouting roll (+2D 
(Simple) for the dragon due to imposing presence of its mate) to detect Aurrdu). Gwenllian wants her 
Eaglestone back. 
Choices 
Obvious choices include use of Misdirection, Strength, Agility and the Eaglestone itself. 
Consequences 
The most effective tactic will be for the PCs to temporarily disable one of the dragons with the Eaglestone 
Amulet, whilst one of their number then sprints into the mound to deposit the remaining Amulet into the 
well at the centre. 
Various combinations of misdirection, armed conflict, agility, Eaglestone as bait, Eaglestone as offensive 
weapon all may work also.  
 
100 For Parties that have not read the Codex Matris (A04), then a kind GM may give the PCs a Lore test at this point. 
If passed, then tell them that the Eaglestones may be used to harm dragons by the holder calling out the Christian 
Lord’s name three times. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
143 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Rooting Interest 
Now the PCs should be intent on getting the Eaglestone Amulet into the well. 
Next Steps 
If the Eaglestone Amulet is returned into the Well of the World at the centre of the L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu 
mound, the well fills will a golden light and then the Pattern Stone above it slowly descends to cover the 
aperture. The PCs feel themselves becoming lighter as their burdens ease (Equivalent to a Quarter Day’s 
Resting, with all Broken conditions removed and one point restored to the relevant Attribute). Venturing 
outside, the PCs will find the Dragons gone and a cooling rain is falling. Move to The Resolution (pg 143). 
The Resolution 
Setting the Scene 
Once the Grim has been laid to rest, the Eaglestone Amulet restored to the Well of the World, or Cernun-
nos’ Torc returned to him, then the signs of The Blight will immediately begin to diminish. The relative 
power of the Old Gods and the New Gods has returned to equilibrium and the land of Gwynedd returns 
to normalcy… for a time.  
The PCs should see signs of regeneration, new growth, returning prosperity, and improving relations 
amongst the factions in Gwynedd. However, the consequences of the PCs’, and others’, actions during The 
Blight remain. Tensions will remain high between Tobrecan and Cadwallon, or even still be in open conflict. 
Bodhmall and Efrog(C6.1) may still be in a power struggle for supremacy over the Druid Order. Finally, 
Maelgwn’s actions may have led to a complete diplomatic breakdown between the Christian’s and Cadwal-
lon.  
Next Steps 
The GM may finish the campaign however they want – there are options for new adventures to be experi-
enced within Gwynedd using the existing material, the PCs may want to venture outside of the Kingdom 
and explore more of Britain, or they may want to settle down and enjoy the fruits of their labour. 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
144 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Setting Guide 
Use as much, or as little, of these Setting Details as you wish in your game. If something seems overcom-
plicated or just not fun, then ignore it. If something piques your interest and you want to know more, 
then there are links to sources which will assist you in fleshing out the details or diving further into what is 
known about Dark Ages Gwynedd. 
Opponents 
Opponents can be: 
• 
Beings of the World, undifferentiable from what history tells us existed at that time.  
• 
Beings of Magic, those that exist in folklore, but due to their propensity to decompose after 
death, have left no trace and are unknown to modern science.  
• 
Beings of Annwn, also from folklore, but from the spirit world and not seen in Gwynedd prior 
to The Blight. 
Beings of the World 
Beings of the World are split into humans of various professions and the animals that inhabit the land. 
Men and Women 
- 
Aillt Farmers – [Strength 4, Agility 3, Wits 2, Empathy 3. Skills: Animal Handling 1, Crafting 2, 
Endurance 2, Healing 1, Melee 2. Talents: Path of the Beast 2. Gear: Spear, D6 silver] 
- 
Ambaxtos Warriors – [Strength 4, Agility 4, Wits 3, Empathy 2. Skills: Might 3, Melee 3, Manip-
ulation 1, Move 1. Talents: Path of the Blade 1. Gear: A Sword, Leather Tunic, D6 silver] 
- 
Druid – [Strength 2, Agility 3, Wits 4, Empathy 3. Skills: Melee 2, Lore 2, Manipulation 2, Move 
1, Healing 1. Talents: Path of Healing or Sight 2. Gear: staff and loose robes, D6 silver] 
- 
Christian Ascetic (Monk or Nun) – [Strength 2, Agility 3, Wits 3, Empathy 4. Skills: Melee 1, Lore 
2, Insight 2, Manipulation 3, Move 1, Healing 1. Talents: Incorruptible 2. Gear: knife, loose robes, 
D6 silver] 
- 
Outlaws – [Strength 3, Agility 3, Wits 2, Empathy 2. Skills: Melee 3, Marksmanship 1, Stealth 2, 
Scouting 1, Survival 2. Talents: Path of the Forest 1. Gear: A Seax, Hand Axe, or Wooden Club, a 
rock, D6 copper] 
- 
Saxon Ċeorls – [Strength 3, Agility 4, Wits 3, Empathy 2. Skills: Animal Handling 1, Crafting 1, 
Insight 2, Melee 2, Manipulation 2. Talents: Path of Words 2. Gear: Spear, D6 silver]  
- 
Traders – [Strength 2, Agility 3, Wits 4, Empathy 3. Skills: Crafting 1, Scouting 2, Sleight of Hand 
2, Insight 1, Manipulation 2. Talents: Path of Lies 2. Gear: knife, backpack, waterskin, map, D6 
silver] 
Animals 
The fauna of Dark Ages Britain includes Bear, Wolf, Fox, Deer, Boar, Riding Horse, War Horse, 
Donkey, Dog, Cat, Rats, Bats, Raven, Eagle, Snake and Spider [Use stats from FLGG pg 126-127] 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
145 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Beings of Magic 
Beings of Magic are well known to the populace of Gwynedd (Though a Lore check can be used to see if 
the PC knows a weakness). These creatures are discussed around the cooking fire, and many have been 
seen by those travelling far from home. All Beings of Magic can communicate in Brittonic and Ango-Saxon. 
All corporeal Beings of Magic quickly begin to decay after death101, any colours fading to grey within 
minutes and the body disintegrating within a few hours or a few weeks (depending on size), leaving only 
Agardite-(Y) crystals behind. These crystals are sought after by Lyblæca Sorcerers because the yttrium when 
combined with glass can be used to create valuable heat and shock-resistant glass weapons (See Bitor (A01)). 
A Lyblæca will pay its weight in silver coins for the substance. In terms of quantities, a Bwbach body will 
create 1D6 silver worth of crystals if allowed to decompose, whilst a Giant might create 6d12 silver worth.  
Bwbachod (boo-BA-cod) (s. Bwbach (BOO-bach)) (Goblins) – Although some think them harmless, the 
Bwbachod are murderous in intent, cunning in nature, and will work cooperatively to take down lone trav-
ellers and even larger groups journeying in the wilds. They are short, perhaps 1m tall, with pronounced 
noses and ears, and folds of thick grey skin over expressionless black eyes. They tend to wear rough skins 
and use clubs and rocks, though their leaders may use stolen seaxes and knives. Bwbachod are fixated 
with the shininess of gold, although they discard the quickly tarnishing silver and copper coins they come 
across. They live in communal nests where they squeeze dozens into small caves and crevices. They are 
nocturnal and sunlight hurts their eyes (-1D to attacks in sunlight) [Use Goblin stats, (FLGG pg 71-72) except 
they have 1D6-4 Gold Coins]  
Cewri (KAY-O-ri) (s. Cawr (COW) (Giants) – Welsh giants appear as a large native Briton, anywhere from 
3 to 4m tall. They wear rough, patchwork clothing and armour, and wield enormous clubs or small trees, 
and can throw large stones (treat as Long Range, Base 9, Dam 2). [Use Giant stats, LFGG pg 96-97] 
Cythraul (KITH-ry-el) (Fiend) – Huge, hairy, ogre-like goblins standing about 2m tall, the Cythraul inhabit 
damp caves and old ruins. They are nomadic, tribal, and avoid sunlight, although it does not badly affect 
their capabilities. Their speaking of human tongues is guttural and course. They have extremely acute hear-
ing and smell, making it difficult to surprise them. Their presence causes magic to behave in erratic ways; 
any Magic Effect within Near Range to a Cythraul will suffer a Magical Mishap on a 2 or 3 as well as a 1 
(bane). Additionally, if the spell succeeds without a mishap, then the effect will be the Opposite to that 
intended102. They are also fascinated by Christians, especially to eat their flesh – their strong sense of smell 
allows them to follow a Christian for miles. [Strength 15, Agility 4, Movement 2, Armour 2, Melee 2, Move 1, Stealth 
2, Scouting 5, Hunting Instincts Talent, Motivation: to be left alone, Claws (7D, D6: 1-2 = 1 dam, 3-5 = 2 dam, 6 = 3 dam, 
Arm’s length), Roar (Fear Attack 8D))] 
Dreigiau (drey-GI-yeh) (Dragon) – Not the Great Red Dragon of legend, but smaller, more bestial of 
mind103, and the apex predator in Gwynedd. To provide them enough food, their territory must cover 
several Cantrefs, and there are just two specimens in Gwynedd, Gwenllian and her mate Aurddu who 
dwell on L3.4 Carnedd y Cawr [Use Small Dragon stats, (FLGG pg 88-89)] The visual difference between 
the two dragons is that Aurddu (Dark Gold) has dark blue scales and Gwenllian (Flaxen Fair) has shim-
mering radiant scales. 
Pwca (POO-ka) (Púca) - are small goblin-like beings who lead lone travellers off the beaten path at night 
using faerie-fire. Their main threat is that of misdirection and fear [Strength 1, Agility 2, Movement 1, Armour 0, 
 
101 Although the large ones, like Giants, can take a little longer to fully decompose. 
102 Whichever plausible effect which will achieve the opposite of that intended. E.g. an offensive spell affects the 
caster, or an augury provides dangerously false information.  
103 Although still capable of limited speech. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
146 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Melee 1, Move 1, Stealth 2, Fast, Can Speak, Scared of Loud Noises, Motivated by fun, Unarmed (Dam 1, blunt, 2D), Willow-
the-Wisp lights (Fear Attack 4D))] 
Beings of Annwn 
Beings of Annwn were rarely encountered before The Blight, and a Demanding (-1d) Lore check will be 
required to see if a PC is aware of accurate details of a type. All Beings of Annwn return there upon their 
defeat, their bodies fading away over a period of minutes. The humanoid-shaped Beings of Annwn can 
communicate in Brittonic and Saxon. 
Afanc (AV-ank) – a monstrous, scaly, reptilian creature with a long, segmented tail that inhabits lakes. They 
prey upon anyone who goes to the waters’ edge. [Strength 8, Agility 8, Movement 2, Armour 3, Camouflaged (-3 
to detect with Scouting), Territorial (the lake), Fangs (12D, D6: 1-2 = 1 dam, 3-5 = 2 dam, 6 = 3 dam, Arm’s length), Tail 
(11D, 1 damage, Near), Sweeping Claw Attack affecting 2 PCs (11D, 1 dam + knocked prone, near), Death Grapple & 
Roll (11D, 1 dam, then Grappled, near, the Afanc will then attempt to drown the character (Drowning FLPG pg 113), 
The existing grapple is considered a free extra attack. Talent: At Home in the Water. Whilst engaged much of its bulk is 
below water, -2D to all melee and ranged attacks trying to strike it)] 
Brenin Llwyd (BRE-nin SHOYD) (Grey King) – a tall silent semi-corporeal figure, cloaked in mist, which 
preys on unwary travellers. Its head shows an obvious mouth, but there is nothing where its eyes should 
be. It usually remains unseen in the mist, its only evidence being the crunching of gravel as it follows its 
prey. [Use Bloodling stats, except Iron affects them once corporeal, FLGG pg 78-79] 
Cŵn Annwn (COON AN-non) – spectral white hounds with blood covered ears. Their misshapen mouths 
are full of thin, crescent-shaped, translucent teeth. They appear to flicker as they approach, disappearing 
and reappearing in little jumps. They leave no tracks. They are of the Otherworld, guard the approached to 
Annwn, and bring ill omens. They will travel with the Wild Hunt, and in their own packs their hunting 
grounds include L5.8 Moel Yscydion. Their growling is loudest when they are at a distance, and as they 
draw nearer, it grows softer and softer. Their coming is generally seen as a death portent. Cŵn Annwn are 
unable to cross running water.  [Use Nightwargs stats, FLGG pg 114-115] 
Deri-Ednyf (De-RI Edd-NEEV)(Oak Essence) – These are Oak Tree Spirits in the service of Cernunnos. 
These being slumber in sacred groves near places of earth power, such as L4.2 Bryn Ceilli Ddu. They appear 
as large oak trees, but when awakened, their roots are ripped from the ground, and they become mobile 
and agitated. [Use Ent stats, FLGG pg 92-93] 
Ellyllon (eh-SHEH-shon) (s. Ellyll) – very small pixie-like elves of the Tylwyth Teg, which are mostly 
shorter than 1/3m tall, grubby, ill-clothed, pointy-eared, and naked, but with very large, teeth-filled mouths 
for their size. Their eyes glitter and their teeth will click as they approach their prey. They are thought to be 
mischievous, but in reality, they hunt in teeming packs, overwhelming their prey with their numbers, sharp 
teeth, and magical hallucinations. When first approached their speech is usually heard as children’s nonsense 
talk, but once closer their victims will hear snatches of language like “come with us”, “give in, you know you want 
to”, “leave her with us, you may leave”, “feed us with your bones”, and similar. They will typically use Entice, 
Paralyze, and Illusion to get their victims to a place where they can swarm the victim and begin to feed. 
[Swarm: Strength 5, Agility 3, Skills & Talents (Move 3, Path of Signs 2), Movement 2, Weapon (Bite, 4D, Damage 1, 
Type Slash)].  
Grim – A corporeal spirit created by sacrificing an animal or person and burying them under the founda-
tions of a Christian church. Appears as they did in life, but with horribly extended and twisted claws and 
eyes that glow a malevolent red [Strength 5, Agility 5, Movement 2, Armour 0, Melee 5, Stealth 3, Move 3, Scouting 2,  
Undead, Regenerating, Fast, Territorial, Claws (8D, D6: 1-2 = 1 dam, 3-5 = 2 dam, 6 = 3 dam, Arm’s length) and Lethal

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
148 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Gwynedd Society 
The kingdom of Gwynedd in 495 CE is a mesh of small provinces, Cantrefs. In the wake of the departure 
of Rome, four generations ago, a great leader arose, Cunedda from the north. Cunedda drove the Hiberni-
ans out of the land and made this area his undisputed territory. After his death 35 years ago the formerly 
united Gwynedd was splintered into tiny kingdoms, ruled by his descendants. Now these small kings, Rhiau, 
are cousins and brothers.  
The culture here is a web of obligations and reciprocal rights. Rhiau jostle for influence. They must attract 
mighty warriors to their cause through the promise of reiving, plunder, and excitement, or they risk being 
dominated by their neighbours.  
Into the vacuum left by Rome has entered the Saxons from the East and the Hibernians from the West. 
Their presence is small but increasing. Also, although the Roman missionaries are long gone, their influence 
remains. Christianity is slowly taking root and displacing the Celtic and Saxon pagan religions. 
Now that the technical powerhouse that is Rome is gone, coins are no longer minted. Roman Solidus and 
even older Celtic Stater coins can still be found but are little used, and barter is more common now. Without 
coin, then taxes (render) are a cut of whatever a person creates; as a result, they are often perishable, so a 
Rhi may need to travel extensively through the year, literally feasting on his due.  
The Druids are a shadow of their former selves, but they still hold significant power. See the Druid profes-
sion (pg. 18) and Druidic Tenets and Reputation (pg. 24).  
The Social Hierarchy 
• 
Rhi (pl. Rhiau), the king of a Cantref 
• 
Cyngran, prince 
• 
Ambaxtos, vassal warriors to a Rhi 
• 
Uchelwr (pl. Uchelwyr), landed nobles  
• 
Aillt (pl Eillt), tenant farmers, including the Ener, or headman/headwoman 
• 
Rhagwas under-servant 
• 
Caeth (pl Caethion), Slaves  
The residents of most trefs are largely composed of Eillt farmers and Caethion slaves. The royal courts of 
each Cantref also will feature Ambastos warriors and Ulchelwyr nobles. 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
149 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Theology 
The Celtic and Saxon polytheistic religions differed; the Celtic Religion emphasised the forces of nature, 
whilst the Saxon Religion is more animistic in basis, with a landscape populated by spirits and non-human 
entities. Regardless, in both cases the gods are part of everyday life. They are prayed to at mealtimes, when 
crops are sown, and when the harvest is reaped. Their capricious nature can affect anyone and everyone, 
but the freedom of expression is a core aspect to their worship. 
Meanwhile Christianity, as brought here by Roman Missionaries, as a monotheistic religion differed in many 
ways. The Christian ideals of purity, chastity, and obedience, stand in stark contrast to that of the Celts and 
the Saxons. A central tenet is that of the Last Judgement – the Christians are literally expecting the end of 
the world at any time. 
Christianity in the 6th Century in Britain disappeared in the East, but continued elsewhere. Around this time 
the first monasteries and convents began to appear, as the idea of religious communities took hold.  In 
Britain the Pelagic Heresy, which denies the concept of original sin, has been churning away for nearly a 
century.  
Every cantref has Christians and Christian influence; however, it is currently much stronger in some areas 
than others.  
Religious Festivals 
Almost nothing is more important than the seasons and their impact on the day-to-day life for those who 
dwell in Gwynedd. The seasons are marked by eight festival days – four associated with equinoxes and 
solstices, and four seasonal markers. These are all celebrated with an intensity that transcends mundane 
marking of the seasons. 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
150 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Festivals in Gwynedd 
The Wheel of the Year: 
1. Samhain, “Assembly” – Start of Winter 
2. Yule - Midwinter Sun Standing (Winter Solstice) 
3. Imbolc, “in the belly” (Feast of Brigid) – Start of Spring 
4. Alban Cyntefin (Saxon: Ēostre) - Martius Equal Night (Spring Equinox) 
5. Bealtaine, “bright fire” - Start of Summer 
6. Liða - Midsummer Sun Standing (Summer Solstice) -  
7. Lunasa, “Calends of August” – Start of Harvest 
8. Alban Elfed - September Equal Night (Autumn Equinox) 
Each of the Eight Festival Days is devoted to feasting and celebration. The rituals provide a centrepiece 
to the Brythonic peoples – the awe and fervour that is generated by massed people carrying out formal 
ceremonies, provides a tangible link to the divine and can provide explanations for an otherwise incompre-
hensible world.  
Each feast day would be anticipated by the preparing of food, selecting sacrificial animals, and prioritising 
tie to gather and celebrate the year-changes in a manner that is literally a matter of life and death. The druids, 
as celebrants, form a core part of the process and their participation ensures an orderly transition of the 
seasons. To interrupt this sacred series is to invite disaster. 
An example of using these festivals would be to include a scene on the top of L3.8 Mynydd Y Cilgwyn, in 
Arfon, during Alban Cyntefin (Spring Equinox) celebrations. 
Deities 
This comprises an incomplete and brief description of some of the deities worshiped by the denizens of 
Gwynedd. 
Brittonic and Gaelic 
Andraste 
Andraste is a goddess of slaughter and war, for whom the hare is sacred. Her name means “unconquerable”.  
Brigid 
Brigid (BREE-gid) is the goddess of spring, fertility, and life. She is also the master of healing and smithing. 
Cernunnos 
Cernunnos (ker-NUN-nos) is a god of animals, nature, and fertility, he is often portrayed with a stag and 
the ram-horned serpent. Cernunnos has protected this area since the Britons began settling the area between 
500 and 200 BCE.  
Note: Cernunnos’ Torc (A02) is heavy gold object and a symbol of his power. Gwyn ap Nudd has sought 
to limit Cernunnos’ power by keeping his torc in Caer Sidi, in Annwn, where no immortal can touch it 
without losing some of their immortality. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
151 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Gwyn ap Nudd 
Gwyn ap Nudd is ruler of Annwn and King of the Tylwyth Teg, the fae. He is the one who leads the Wild 
Hunt on his demon horse – the hunt is made up of his Cŵn Annwn hounds (See Beings of Annwn (pg. 146)). 
Nodens 
Nodens is a god of healing. Known as the Wealthy One and The Catcher, he is associated with dogs and 
hunting, although for some he is the vengeful god of the sea. 
Saxon 
Wōden 
Wōden (Odin), is the head of the Saxon pantheon. He is associated with wisdom, healing, death, royalty, 
the gallows, knowledge, war, battle, victory, sorcery, poetry, and frenzy. He is the husband of the goddess 
Frigg.  
Tīw 
Tīw is the god of war, and central to the pantheon.  
Þunor 
Þunor (Thor) is son of Wōden and husband to Sibb, he is known for his relentless slaughter of his foes and 
fierce battles with the monstrous serpent Wyrmsele. 
Christian God 
The Christian God of the 6th Century is a monotheistic god, involves the trinity of the Father, the Son, and 
Holy Spirit, is at once Human and Divine, allows for Atonement and Resurrection, and is a god who will 
be returning for the final judgement of man.  
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
152 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Druid Rituals 
The Oak and the Mistletoe 
This is carried out as described by Pliny the Elder:  
Mistletoe is rare and when found it is gathered with great ceremony, and particularly on the sixth day of the moon.... 
Hailing the moon in a native word that means 'healing all things,' they prepare a ritual sacrifice and banquet beneath 
an oak tree and bring up two white bulls, whose horns are bound for the first time on this occasion. A priest arrayed 
in white vestments climbs the tree and, with a golden sickle, cuts down the mistletoe, which is caught in a white cloak. 
Then finally they kill the victims, praying to a god to render his gift propitious to those on whom he has bestowed it. 
They believe that mistletoe given in drink will impart fertility to any animal that is barren and that it is an antidote 
to all poisons. 
The resulting elixir provides an antidote to the four types of poison (FLPG pg 113-114). Number of doses 
produced is at the GM’s discretion. 
Druidic Initiation – a dance with Annwn 
This ceremony takes place in a secluded grove surrounded by ancient, towering oaks. The grove is consid-
ered sacred, as the veil is thin here. Initiates finish the ceremony as fully accepted druids, leaving their 
previous lives behind. 
Attire: Initiates don simple, earth-toned robes. Each initiate wears a circlet made of intertwined plants and 
flowers. 
Preliminary Rituals: 
Purification: Initiates undergo a purification ritual involving a cleansing bath in a nearby sacred spring, 
symbolizing the shedding of the mundane world, and embracing spiritual rebirth. 
Anointment: The initiates are anointed with oils crafted from sacred herbs, enhancing their connection to 
the earth's energies. 
Central Rite: 
Gathering of Magic Mushrooms: Under the guidance of elder Druids, initiates embark on a journey deeper 
into the grove to collect mushrooms known for their hallucinogenic properties. These mushrooms are 
carefully chosen for their connection to the Otherworld and the enhancement of spiritual perception. 
Communion with the Spirits: Back in the centre of the grove, a large cauldron is prepared. The gathered 
mushrooms are brewed into a potent concoction, and the initiates, along with the elder Druids, partake in 
a communal ingestion of the magical elixir. As the effects take hold, the boundaries between the physical 
and spiritual realms blur. 
Dance of the Elements: Accompanied by rhythmic drumming and chanting, the initiates engage in a cere-
monial dance representing the elements—earth, air, fire, and water. This dance is believed to attune the 
participants to the energies of the natural world and the divine. 
Oracle Seekers: In the heightened state of consciousness induced by the mushrooms, initiates may experi-
ence visions, receive insights, and commune with the spirits of the Otherworld. The elder Druids guide 
them through this mystical journey, interpreting the signs and symbols that emerge. 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
153 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Conclusion: 
As the effects of the magic mushrooms begin to wane, the initiates are led to a sacred stone circle within 
the grove. Here, they are symbolically reborn into the Druidic order, their newfound wisdom and connec-
tion to the Otherworld acknowledged by the elders. 
Emendigaid 
Emendigaid is Cursing. A high-status druid (Reputation 5+) may curse anyone by inscribing the name of 
the cursed, details of the curse, and an entreaty to a relevant pagan god. The inscription may be on a stone, 
piece of wood, or piece of lead or other metal, and pieces of hair or clothing may be included to increase 
the potency. See Druidic Tenets and Reputation (pg 24) for details.  
Funerals 
The practices for disposing of the dead fluctuated wildly in these times, from cremation and storage of the 
ashes in pots to burials, inhumation, in various poses (crouched, laid in various directions), and back again.  
Further east the prevailing practice has been inhumation for the last 20 years, but in Gwynedd, we presume, 
that with the Romans formal departure nearly 
100 years before, the local pagans have returned 
to the old ways, influenced by the Saxon elites. 
Now the dead are burned on funeral pyres and 
their ashes are buried in crudely kilned pots.  
This practice has only accelerated with the ad-
vent of the Plague and the desire to purify their 
loved ones’ remains.   
Christians on the other hand have kept the Ro-
man ways and insist on inhumation, the bodies 
buried with their head to the west.   
Magic & Visions 
Auguries and Visions 
A core theme for this Campaign is that Magic is only aware of the present, not the future nor past. The 
future is just a set of probabilities, whilst the past only exists in the memories of those witnesses (human, 
animal, or otherwise) who remain in the present.  
Therefore, any foretelling of the future (e.g. Divination) will come back with ambiguous answers which can 
be read in different ways, and any questioning of the past (e.g. Visions of the Past) will reflect what people 
or animals remember of the event and be subject to their own biases and errors104. This means that those 
looking into the past can only go as far back as the memories of witnesses or what may have been recorded 
in oral or written tradition. 
 
104 In the case of only animal witnesses, then what is remembered will likely be extremely basic and garbled.

The PCs will likely look to divination to answer specific questions regarding their quest. E.g. what should 
they do with Cernunnos’ Torc once they have obtained it? For these types of questions, then the answer 
from the augury should be to point the PCs towards the person or location that can supply the answer.  
If more general questions are asked then the GM may find the Divination Oracle, below, useful. The 
Divinations are intended to use ambiguous terms with double meanings, and therefore reflect the truth 
which is only understood in hindsight. All the items in the table reflect a truth about where the Kingdoms 
are going or where they may be going depending on the actions of the PCs. 
If a PC carries out a Divination asking a question about the subject of one of the Truths, ie. Asking a 
question regarding the relationship between the keywords105, then give them the related Divination.

Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Divination Methods 
Some methods that may be used by Druids and other diviners: 
• 
Cetnad, repeating verses – The Diviner journeys to an isolated hilltop where they fast for several 
days. They then begin repeating a mantra over and over for several hours. Eventually they enter an 
ecstatic state where truths are seen.  
• 
Crannchur, the casting of the woods – The Diviner throws a handful of chits in the air, each 
marked with a rune. The pattern that they create when they land in a sacred bowl is read and 
provides the augury.  
• 
Neldoracht, direction from clouds – The Diviner drinks a potion made from fly agaric and then 
lies back on the ground and watches the shapes of the clouds overhead. 
• 
Tarbhfeis, bull dream – The druid consumes the flesh of a white bull, recites an invocation to the 
gods, and then lies on their back, covers their eyes with their hands, and partakes of a visionary 
sleep. 
Note: A druid giving an augury will roll their eyes into the back of their head, give out ululating shrieks, and 
spin “sunwise” (clockwise).  
General Effects and Power Zones 
Use the Forbidden Lands rules for magic (FLPG pg 116-142) but make the following general modifications. 
See below for a map of Zones in Gwynedd. 
1. Those using True Sight (FLPG pg 128) will 
also notice that ghostly apparitions seem to 
be drawn to the living in places there has 
been death and destruction, and that in the 
vicinity of sacred sites (Power Zone 1) there 
appears to be ghostly versions of standing 
stones and cairns that cannot be seen with 
the naked eye. 
2. The following applies to Druids and Sorcer-
ers only: 
a. 
Near pagan sacred sites, Power Zone 1, 
the Power Level is increased by 1. 
b. 
In Christian areas, Power Zone 2, the 
Power Level is reduced by 1 (Minimum 
total Power Level of 1).  
c. 
Near Christian sacred sites, Power Zone 
3, the Power Level is reduced by 2 (Min-
imum total Power Level of 1). 
d. 
Outside of Power Zones 1 through 3 
magic operates without modification.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
158 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Grimoires 
Only Lyblæca use Grimoires.  
Christian Ascetics with Path of Saints (3) do not have access to Grimoires or Ingredients, their powers are 
granted directly by their god. 
Druids only pass on knowledge orally, but the same mechanical effect of a Grimoire is achieved using an 
Adder Stone, a “Glain Neidr”. These small glassy stones with naturally occurring holes are created by a 
congress of snakes, which are overseen whilst the druid intones the spell (Has the same pre-requisites as 
Grimoire creation, requires a Lore roll, and takes the same amount of time). Once created, then any Druid 
may access the relevant spells by holding the Glain Neidr (Readied in combat the same as a Grimoire). 
New and Altered Spells 
As a rule of thumb, any spell or mishap that involves demons and other planes in Forbidden Lands System 
mechanics, now involves Beings of Annwn and Annwn. For the spells: 
General: Open the Ways (2) will grant access at a Senex Vaie node and allow the caster to know the right 
path to the desired exit node. Related to the Portal (3) Symbolism spell which can create a portal into 
Annwn at any location. 
Awareness: True Sight (1) (FLPG pg 128) acts as normal, but with the additional effect that people with 
a magical talent glow slightly and that the Senex Vaie nodes can be seen as areas where ghostly standing 
stones bristle, and ghostly brakes of trees can be seen beside the path. Instead of a Magnifying Glass the 
ingredients are the (fresh) eyes of a bird. 
Awareness: Visions of the Past (2) acts as normal, but the aspect “if events are no longer remembered by any 
living being” is overridden by Xauguries and visionsX above. 
Awareness: Tynged (3), Range: Unlimited, Duration: D66 days, Ingredients: an object owned by the sub-
ject. This spell applies a Tynged, a geas, on one subject. The Tynged can take the following forms.   
• 
Curse. This is mechanically equivalent to Blood Curse (3) 
• 
Taboo. The subject is given a set of restrictions (one restriction per use of the spell). If the subject 
holds to the taboo restrictions, then they will receive a boon. However, if they break the taboo, 
then they will suffer consequences. The restrictions must be setting appropriate (eg. The subject 
cannot turn back once they start a journey; they must not refuse a challenge; and they must never 
tell anyone their name.) and can allow for situations where the subject must break the Tynged106. 
Whilst the subject holds to the restrictions all related Skill and Combat rolls are at +1D, but if they 
break any restrictions then ALL Skill and Combat rolls are at -2D until the spell duration is met.    
• 
Fate. The subject must be told their fated death. E.g. you will die by the hand of a Gael. Whenever 
the subject is in a situation where the Fate may come true, say confronted by said Gael, then all 
their skill and combat rolls are at -2D.  
If the Taboo or Fate versions of a Tynged is applied to a PC, then they can choose to try to resist the spell. 
This requires an Insight roll with a negative modification equal to the Power Level of the spell. 
 
106 For example, the geasa on Irish hero, Cú Chulainn, that he must never eat the meat of a dog, nor refuse food 
offered by a woman. His enemies conspired to have an old woman offer him dog meat, he was forced to break one 
geas or the other, and he died the next day.

Healing: Banish Demon (2) (FLPG pg 123) is replaced by Banish Being of Annwn (2). Text is the same 
but replace all instances of “Demon(s)” and “Demonic” with “Being(s) of Annwn”. 
Blood Magic: Scent of Blood (1), Range: Long, Duration: 1 turn, Ingredient: needle. This spell can detect 
the living through the flow of blood in their veins. Only a general direction and distance is possible, not 
precise locations. It will also tell the caster if the targets are male or female. This only works on living 
creatures. 
Blood Magic: Replace Immolate (2) with Curdle (2), Range: Near, Duration: Immediate, Ingredient: Small 
Cup of Blood. This causes the blood to thicken, to grip the organs like a vice. The recipient is wracked with 
pain. This spell inflicts damage to AGI equal to the Power Level. You can choose to completely immobilise 
the target for that turn by spending an extra Willpower (which does not count towards Power Level). This 
spell only works against living humanoids. 
Blood Magic: Bind Demon (2) (FLPG pg 137) is replaced by Bind Being of Annwn (2). Text is the same 
but replace all instances of “Demon(s)” and “Demonic” with “Being(s) of Annwn”.

Note: Roman items since their withdrawal from these isles are irreplaceable, valuable, and mysterious. 
A01. Bitor (Agardite Glass Sword) 
Location: L4.6 Sancta Arcis 
Created by Tobrecan y Nerth(C4.4) by casting glass with the crystal remains of giants and Bwbachod (Agard-
ite-Y). This crystalline sword retains a wicked edge. The sword is 0.7m total length, much thicker than an 
iron or streel weapon, with a blade, cross-piece, tang, and pommel of a single piece of red-tinted glass. The 
hilt is glass wrapped in leather, the pommel is a clear glass sphere, and otherwise the sword is unadorned.  
Effects: As Broadsword with Artifact Die D8.  
Drawbacks: If the weapon is damaged the glass shatters, possibly cutting both combatants (One Base Die 
D4 attack of Damage 1),  
A02. Cernunnos’ Torc 
Location: L3.7 Llyn Gwynant and Caer Sidi 
A heavy gold torc with stags’ heads figured at each end.  
Effects: On donning the torc, magnificent rack of antlers sprout from the wearer’s temples. All Attributes 
are increased by 2D. The antlers act as an 10D, 2 Damage, stab attack.    
Drawbacks: The longer the torc is worn the less human-thinking the wearer becomes. Mechanical effect – 
each time it is donned the wearer’s Wits are reduced by 1 die, to a minimum of 2 dice. Wits are restored at 
a rate of 1 die each 1D6 days.  
A03. Codex Climaci Rescriptus 
Location: L4.6 Sancta Arcis 
This palimpsest codex (pages reused multiple times) was originally parts of the Old and New testament and 
Apocryphal tales, written in Christian Palestinian Aramaic on papyrus with a simple leather-over-wood 
cover boards. The original text has been erased and overwritten with spells of Blood Magic, Death Magic, 
and Symbolism. 
Effect: Grimoire with 
Blood Magic (Blood Curse (2), Bind Being of Annwn (2), Curdle Blood (2)),  
Death Magic (Raise the Dead (2), Speak to the Dead (2), Steal Life(3), Terror (3)),  
Symbolism (Puppeteer (3), Power Rune (2), Portal (3)) 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
161 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
A04. Codex Matris 
Location: L4.7 Ynys Lannog 
The Book of the Mother is a codex containing the apocryphal Gospel of Mary.  The central figure is a Mary 
(possibly the Mother, Sister or Wife of Jesus), who is comforting and encouraging the disciples at the time 
of Jesus’ crucifixion. The book is written in velum with leather covered wood boards encrusted in semi-
precious stones. The writing inside is simple text in Greek without illustrations or adornment, though the 
ink is an unusual red-brown and the velum doesn’t look like calf skin. The Codex also includes a passage 
on the Eaglestones: 
And Mary spoke, saying, "Hearken unto me, children of the God, for the wisdom of the ages is upon you. The 
Eaglestones, known as geodes, are treasures from the womb of the virgin mother. They are born of her but formed in 
the earth, holding within them the power to restore and renew. Wyrms covet and fear them, for if the name of Our 
Lord is called three times over an Eaglestone in their presence, then such a beast under your focus will lose its senses. 
 
When the land grows weary and the soil is barren, it is a sign that the balance has been disturbed. To heal the land, 
you must return an Eaglestone to the well of their making, for in this act of reverence, the cycle of life is honoured, 
and the earth's vitality is renewed. The deep waters shall embrace the stone, and from this union, new life shall spring 
forth. 
 
Yet, there is a ruler whose heart is hardened and whose greed has ravaged the land. His tyranny has plundered the 
land’s bounty and hoarded the stone for his own gain. In righteous fury, the time has come to enact divine retribution. 
He shall be cast down from his throne, and his dominion shall crumble. For it is written that those who defile the 
sacred must face the consequences of their deeds. 
 
The people shall rise with righteous anger, and justice shall be their guide. The king will be punished, and the 
Eaglestone will be returned to its rightful place. Thus, the earth shall be healed, and harmony shall be restored, as 
the Creator intended. Amen." 
Effects: The holder of the Codex gets +2D to all Lore checks, as the wisdom of Christ fills them. 
Drawbacks: The holder often has nightmares of the crucifixion. They soon get used to this, but the first 
night after obtaining the book they do not rest properly and are Sleepy the next day.  
A05. Brooch of Cúchulainn 
Location: The Mines of L6.1 Cyngreawdr Fynydd 
A fine gold brooch showing a Gael charioteer, which can be used to fix a cloak or a woman’s peplos. 
Effects: the wearer gains the Beserker (3) talent 
A06. Eaglestone Amulet 
Location: Dragon’s lair on L3.4 Carnedd y Cawr 
Eaglestones are named so because Eagles are thought to take them to their nest to facilitate egg-laying. The 
dragon collects these walnut-sized stones, and one can help bring back fertility to the land and drive off 
The Blight. The amulet consists of a large Eaglestone wrapped in bronze and gold twine on a leather thong. 
The stone rattles loudly when shaken.   


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
162 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Effects: If worn on the body, then it provided a D8 Artefact die to all Healing tests. If it is left in one place 
over several hours, say during an overnight camp, then plants will begin to sprout from the ground and 
existing plants will begin to bloom.  
Eaglestones also have a debilitating effect if used as a weapon against dragons. The stones will start to 
vibrate and rattle in a Dragon’s presence. If the Eaglestone Amulet is held and the name of the Christian 
God called out Three Times whilst the holder concentrates on one Dragon, then their power over 
Dragons is activated; carry out an opposed Wits test between the holder and the Dragon. If the thrower 
wins the test, then the dragon will drop to its haunches, apparently entranced by the Eaglestone Amulet for 
1D3 rounds107. This becomes harder each time it is attempted, add +2 to the Dragon’s Wits roll each time.  
Drawbacks: The dragons Gwenllian and Aurrdu fear and covet the trove of stones and, above all, the 
Amulet. They will seek to retrieve both from those who take it from them. 
A07. Greal Sanctaidd 
Location: L4.3 Llwyn o Gerrig 
The Holy Grail, not to be confused with the Holy Chalice, is simply a roughly hammered copper bowl, 
which is sacred to the goddess Brigid.  
Effects: If someone subject to a Healing or Recovery test drinks from the Greal Sanctaidd, then add a 
D8 Artefact Die to the roll. The Greal also aids those traversing the Senex Vaie, adding a D8 Artefact Die 
to the Move/Insight rolls of all those navigating the Ways in its presence. 
Drawbacks: The person applying the Healing or Recovery is confronted with visions of Brigid. The effect 
of being confronted with sight of a god is a D4 attack on Wits. 
A08. Lancea Longini 
Location: L3.3 Caer Rhun and Canovium  
The Lancea Longhini, or Spear of Destiny, is a long spear, with an ornate bronze head on an obviously new 
shaft. 
This holy relic is believed to once have been in possession of the Roman Centurion, Gaius Cassius Longi-
nus, at the crucifixion of Jesus Christ. Longinus’ spear is believed to have pierced Jesus’ side on the cross 
and the splashing blood cured the Centurion’s diseased eyesight. 
It is a fake; it has been infused by the faith of Saints, however, and thus gained its power. The Lancea 
Longini was originally held by a Roman Centurion in Canovium in the 1st Century, this was passed down 
from father to son, and when the fortress was abandoned in the 4th Century, a Roman Limitanei who held 
the artefact stayed behind. Although they were not Christians, his family continued to conceal and revere 
it. It is currently hidden along with a Roman Galea Helmet (A13) in an old cistern under the abandoned 
fort of Canovium. 
Effects:  
• 
For every Success that causes damage to an opponent, the wielder recovers one Strength, as the 
opponent’s blood runs down the blade and nourishes the wielder. Artefact die D8.  
• 
The artefact acts to drive off Grims. Any Grim is at -3D to attack a person holding the Lancea 
Longini. 
 
107 This power can only be effective against one dragon at a time. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
163 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Drawbacks: Once the spear has healed its wielder in combat, it will seek to fully restore all Strength. If the 
wielder is not at full Strength when the combat ends, then they will feel a compulsion to attack the nearest 
creature until at least one more point of Strength is restored. The Wielder may resist this compulsion with 
a successful Manipulation (Empathy) test.  
Additional powers: If wielded by a descendent of Cunnedda and Beli Mawr (such as Maelgwn(C3.10)), then 
the Lancea Longini awakes a warrior spirit in the individual that seeks to destroy Christians. They will lash 
out with the artefact, and now the need for Strength is amplified and the compulsion becomes that of taking 
the strength of those of the Christian faith and its thirst is not slaked by blood – they will continue to kill 
with it until they are brought down or no Christians remain.   
A09. Lleuad Cilgant 
Location: held by Emrys Nofather(C6.2) 
The Lleuad Cilgant, the moon sickle, is a battered silver sickle with a rough leather handle. If used to gather 
medicinal herbs, especially at the time of the full moon, the herbs produce healing balms and elixirs of great 
efficacy. 
Effect: Any healing checks carried out using herbs previously gathered using the sickle adds a D8 artefact 
die. If the herbs are gathered under the light of the full moon, it becomes a D10 artefact die.  
A10. Morgen’s Scrying Bowl 
Location: L2.3 Caer Wydyr 
A simple battered silver bowl, but when filled with water it assists druids with the Path of Sight.  
Effect: The operator can use scrying spells from the Path of Sight (Farsight(2), Visions of the Past(2), 
Divination (3) and Intuition(3)) as if they were 1 higher Rank . Additionally, if a short ritual is carried out 
(one turn), an item placed in the bowl can be dedicated to the gods. The item is then transported to Annwn. 
Drawbacks: none 
A11. Pibgorn of Glamour 
Location: Held by Afandreg Mightypipes(C5.1) in L5.6 Gaer Dolbenmaen  
A battered bone pibgorn pipe with a flared horn encased in silver.  
Effects: Music created with this instrument is so sweet that it adds a D10 Artefact die to any Performance 
test, and additionally any effective WP spend on Path of the Hymn, Song and Warcry is doubled. 
A12. Roman Lorica Lamminata Armour 
Location: L1.4 Tre’r Ceiri 
Late Roman Scale Armour. This body armour is constructed of soft iron and case-
hardened steel plate that have been overlapped and riveted to leather straps.  
Effects: Armour Rating 4, Body, Heavy Item. Artefact die D8 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
164 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
A13. Roman Galea Helmet 
Location: L3.3 Caer Rhun and Canovium 
A Late Roman ridge helmet. A decorated iron helm with cheek plates and a prominent central fore-aft 
ridge. It is inlaid with silver and gold and encrusted with semi-precious gems. It is hidden along with the 
Lancea Longini (A08) in an old cistern under the abandoned fort of Canovium. 
Effects: Armour Rating 4. Head. Modifies Scout by -2. Artefact die D8 
A14. Saint Cynog’s Gilded Armilla 
Location: L3.10 Saint Cynog’s Monastery, Arfon 
Known as the “Torc of Heaven”, this gold-plated iron armilla (arm-bracelet) was originally gifted by the King 
Brychan to his son, Cynog as a child. Now it is held by Abad Cadfan(C3.4) in his monastery.   
Effects: If held in the hand it functions as a Mace with an Artefact die D10. 
A15. Sonio Grwyn (talk rattles) 
Location: one held by Tobrecan’s(C4.4) warlord Scáthach(C4.3) and the other by her lieutenant, Gofan ap 
Gareth(C4.1). 
This pair of small rattles, made of dried skin and filled with stones, can be used to communicate warnings 
at great distance. If one holder shakes their rattle vigorously, then the other rattle will sound also. Otherwise, 
they make a dull thudding sound when shaken, which does not pass to the other.  
 
 
 
 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
165 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
Strongholds 
Old, abandoned, iron-age hillforts litter the land108. They are in varying states of repair, but the PCs should 
be able to find one with existing Ramparts with little difficult. It might even have a Dungeon, Guard Tower, 
dry Moat, Root Cellar, Vault, and/or Well amongst its functions. Some examples, which could be relocated, 
include: 
Hillforts (Wikipedia list) 
• 
Garn Bentyrch (megalithic), in Afflogion and about 6km to the southeast of Tre’r Ceiri Hillfort; it 
would already have Ramparts, a dry Moat, and a holy Well109 when the PCs arrive to take it over 
{93532}.  
• 
Castell Odo, on the summit of Mynydd Ystum, near Aberdaron. {95305}. 
• 
Craig yr Aderyn, on the dramatic Birds Rock, although it is to the south of Dunoding, its details 
could be reused for a more central location. {302862} 
Abandoned Roman Forts 
• 
Tomen y Mur, in Dunoding, a significant abandoned Roman fort east of Gaer Dolbenmaen 
{95476}. 
• 
Caer Llugwy, in Arvon, another Roman fort between Canovium and Tomen y Mur {95274}. 
• 
Pen Llystyn, in Dunoding, 4 km to the northwest of Gaer Dolbenmaen. Its wooden palisade would 
have been long fallen, requiring new ramparts {301056}. 
 
 
108 See “An Inventory of Ancient Monuments in Caernarvonshire” Vol II Central and Vol III West, RCAHMWM, for details. 
109 Associated a just a few years later with St Cybi. 


 
 
The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0

Travel 
Travel in the Kingdoms is not straightforward. Most of the old Roman roads are now choked with weeds, 
and few folks have the luxury to travel about, nor the martial strength to take the risks of bandits, and 
worse.  
Unaffiliated wanderers, especially heavily armed and armoured wanderers, will evoke suspicion and distrust. 
Even those bearing the tokens of a Rhi will be viewed with some suspicion if operating outside of that Rhi’s 
cantref.

will usually roll on the random encounter table once every Quarter Day while you HIKE, and once per day 
if you remain in the same hexagon. As the PCs may be traversing several terrain types, then choose the one 
which appears the most appropriate.

The Ways of Blood, Magic, and Spirit, v1.0 
167 
Stephen Dosman (@Dosmania) 
1. Monastic convoy – Christian Ascetics travelling to or from St Cynogs. 
2. Druid procession – Bodhmall’s druids travelling for inscrutable reasons. 
3. The local Rhi’s troops guarding a ford – Their reception will depend on the PCs’ reputation 
and relationship with the Rhi in question. Regardless, if the PCs appear to have wealth, yet betray 
any sign of weakness, then the troops (Ambaxtos Warriors) may try to shake them down.  
4. Hungry Outlaws – More interested in food than anything else. 
5. Plaguebearers – These former Eillt Farmers have been driven out of their Tref and bear a linen 
sheet with a red-brown “X” (The Plague, Virulence 4, Disease, FLPG pg 114). For more infor-
mation see The Plague (pg 32). 
6. Pack of Wolves – howling that gets louder and lounder until a pack of hungry wolves leaps into 
view. 
7. Mischievous Bwbachod – If the PCs carry any gold, the Bwbachod will come sniffing around 
for it. If not, then the goblins may just play a trick on the PCs. 
8. Swarm of Hungry Ellyll (day) or Pwca (night) – During the day the PCs may come across the 
swarm Ellyll feeding on a fellow traveller, or the Ellyll may come across the PCs campsite and use 
their magic to overcome the watchperson. Alternatively at night, a Pwca may try to lure the PCs 
off the path or out of the camp and lead them into difficulty in a quagmire, or similar. 
9. Pack of Water Leapers – Whilst travelling by boat or beside a river the PCs may be beset by these 
leaping monsters. 
10. Drunk Giant – The PCs hear swearing and the sounds and the vibration of thuddering impacts 
before this Giant, very drunk on mead, staggers into view. 
11. Cŵn Annwn – Loud howls that progressively become fainter and fainter until the white hounds 
with blood red ears leap at the PCs. 
12. Gwyllon – This grey, corpse-like woman drags herself along the mountain roads and paths, at-
tracted by the warmth of the PCs’ bodies. 
13. If in the daytime - Dragon – Seen circling far overhead before departing. The sight still gives 
great unease (Fear attack 1D). (After O18. Anger the Dragon this is modified as discussed on page 
131). If at night - The Wild Hunt – seen passing in the distance (Fear attack 1D) (see Gwyn ap 
Nudd (pg 151)).

Rumours Tables 
When interacting with locals in any of the Cantrefs you can roll a D12 to see what rumours they may hear.

Political

Einion any time soon, that one is a staunch Christian if ever there was one, even if they 
are related.”

Einion off on Cadwallon to further his own ends.”

cus. Then my sister, she says that Elno was also seen talking to Cadwallon’s witch,

around!”

tians

“God is not protecting us from the Saxons. Why? I’ll tell you, because we are still a

“My little Aeddan was playing near those standing stones but an 8-day ago. Something 
frightened him terrible-like. That wasn’t what scared ME, though. What scared ME is 
that some Saxon found him a day’s journey away, wandering in [use as appropriate] 
Afflogion/Dunoding/Arfon!”
